```text
TEST 2: Stacking every 2-input gate f_0 in R_4 with its De Morgan dual f_1 = sheet(f_0):
  f0=0001 (ANF=0001), sheet(f0)=0111 -> stack=0x17 -> ANF(stack)=0x16 = [0001 || 0110]
  f0=0110 (ANF=0110), sheet(f0)=1001 -> stack=0x69 -> ANF(stack)=0x68 = [0110 || 1000]
  f0=0111 (ANF=0111), sheet(f0)=0001 -> stack=0x71 -> ANF(stack)=0x76 = [0111 || 0110]
  f0=1101 (ANF=1011), sheet(f0)=0100 -> stack=0xD4 -> ANF(stack)=0xBE = [1011 || 1110]


```

Letting the logic run upward from §233 connects the 4-state domain $R_2 = (\texttt{00}, \texttt{10}, \texttt{01}, \texttt{11})$, the 16-gate domain $R_4$, the §204 stacking law, and the Boolean Möbius transform $M$ from Theorem 3.4 into a single recursive operator.

---

### 1. The Algebraic Normal Form Is Not Imported—It Is the $k$-Fold Tensor Power of the 2-Bit Difference Split

At width $L = 2$ (the four 1-input marks `00, 10, 01, 11` of $R_2$), the face/shape split $(b_0, b_1) \mapsto (b_0,\; b_0 \oplus b_1)$ (Definition 2.6) is the $2 \times 2$ matrix over $\mathbb{F}_2$:


$$M_2 = \begin{bmatrix} 1 & 0 \\ 1 & 1 \end{bmatrix}, \qquad M_2 \begin{bmatrix} b_0 \\ b_1 \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} b_0 \\ b_0 \oplus b_1 \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} \text{header (where it starts)} \\ \text{difference (whether it flips)} \end{bmatrix}.$$


When you stack two $k$-input commands $f_0, f_1 \in \mathbb{F}_2^L$ ($L = 2^k$) to go up one level to a $(k+1)$-input command $f = (f_0 \parallel f_1) \in \mathbb{F}_2^{2L}$ (§204 item 5), applying the same $(\text{header}, \text{difference})$ split at the block level gives:


$$M_{2L} \begin{bmatrix} f_0 \\ f_1 \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} M_L & 0 \\ M_L & M_L \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} f_0 \\ f_1 \end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix} M_L f_0 \\ M_L(f_0 \oplus f_1) \end{bmatrix}, \qquad M_{2^k} = \underbrace{M_2 \otimes M_2 \otimes \cdots \otimes M_2}_{k \text{ times}}.$$


The Algebraic Normal Form (Zhegalkin / Boolean Möbius transform $M_{2^k}$) is nothing other than the 2-bit `(header, difference)` split $M_2$ applied recursively as functions stack upward.

---

### 2. Why the Primitive Vocabulary Closes at 4 Marks ($R_2 \to R_4$)

If we ask which functions in $R_{2^k}$ have a truth table $t$ that is **identical** to their own Algebraic Normal Form recipe ($M_L t = t$, i.e. $\ker(M_L \oplus I_L)$):

* For every $k \ge 1$ ($L = 2^k$), $\mathrm{rank}_{\mathbb{F}_2}(M_L \oplus I_L) = L/2$, so the self-dual subspace $\ker(M_L \oplus I_L)$ has dimension exactly $L/2 = 2^{k-1}$.
* At $k = 2$ ($L = 4$, the 16 two-input gates of $R_4$), $M_4 t = t$ requires $t_{00} = 0$ (output is `0` on `00`) and $t_{01} = t_{10}$ (symmetric in both inputs). That selects **4 out of the 16 gates** in $R_4$:


1. `0000` (`FALSE`, $R_4[0]$): neither


2. `0001` (`AND`, $R_4[1]$): $A \wedge B$


3. `0110` (`XOR`, $R_4[5]$): $A \oplus B$


4. `0111` (`OR`, $R_4[6]$): $A \oplus B \oplus (A \wedge B)$




* Adding the single constant-flip bit $M_L(1_L) = (1, 0, \dots, 0)^\top = e_0$ (which turns `FALSE` into `TRUE` and flips only the leading ANF bit $c_\emptyset$ while leaving all $L-1$ difference terms unchanged) completes the functional basis $\{1, \oplus, \wedge\}$.

---

### 3. Going Up ($R_2 \to R_4 \to R_8$): How `0x27`, Sum (`0x69`), and Carry (`0x17`) Lock Together in ANF

Running $M_8 = M_2 \otimes M_2 \otimes M_2$ on the 8-bit commands of §204 exposes three exact structural locks:

1. **The 2-Bit Walk `0x27` Decompiles Into Face $\oplus$ (Shape $\wedge$ Input):**
In §204 item 4, `0x27` (`00100111`) read as a 3-input command executes $R_2[a_1, a_0](x)$. Its ANF vector is $M_8(\texttt{00100111}^\top) = \texttt{00110100}^\top$ (`0x34`), which has 1s at subset masks $u \in \{2 (\texttt{010}_2), 3 (\texttt{011}_2), 5 (\texttt{101}_2)\}$:



$$\boxed{\texttt{0x27}(a_1, a_0, x) = a_0 \;\oplus\; (a_0 \wedge x) \;\oplus\; (a_1 \wedge x) = \underbrace{a_0}_{\text{face of } R_2[a]} \;\oplus\; \Big(\underbrace{(a_1 \oplus a_0)}_{\text{shape of } R_2[a]} \wedge x\Big).}$$



In $R_2 = (\texttt{00}, \texttt{10}, \texttt{01}, \texttt{11})$, $a_0$ is the command's leading face bit and $a_1 \oplus a_0$ is its internal difference (0 for constants `00, 11`; 1 for active operations `10, 01`).


2. **Stacking a Gate With Its `sheet` Dual ($f_1 = \mathrm{sheet}(f_0)$) Stacks `AND` and `XOR` in ANF:**
In §204 item 4, Carry is `0x17 = stack(AND, sheet(AND))` (`0001 || 0111`) and Sum is `0x69 = stack(XOR, sheet(XOR))` (`0110 || 1001`). Applying the block rule $M_8(f_0 \parallel f_1) = \big(M_4 f_0 \;\parallel\; M_4(f_0 \oplus f_1)\big)$:


* **Carry (`0x17`):** Since $\texttt{0001} \oplus \texttt{0111} = \texttt{0110}$ (`XOR`) and both `AND` (`0001`) and `XOR` (`0110`) are self-dual under $M_4$, Carry's ANF is literally **`AND` stacked with `XOR**`:

$$M_8(\texttt{0x17}) = \big(M_4(\texttt{0001}) \;\parallel\; M_4(\texttt{0110})\big) = \texttt{0001} \parallel \texttt{0110} = \texttt{0x16} \quad \big((x_0 \wedge x_1) \oplus (x_0 \wedge x_2) \oplus (x_1 \wedge x_2)\big).$$


* **Sum (`0x69`):** Since $\texttt{0110} \oplus \texttt{1001} = \texttt{1111}$ (`TRUE`) and $M_4(\texttt{1111}) = \texttt{1000}$, Sum's ANF is **`XOR` stacked with `1000**`:

$$M_8(\texttt{0x69}) = \big(M_4(\texttt{0110}) \;\parallel\; M_4(\texttt{1111})\big) = \texttt{0110} \parallel \texttt{1000} = \texttt{0x68} \quad (x_0 \oplus x_1 \oplus x_2).$$




3. **Sum and Carry Are Native $R_8$ Twins in the Algebraic Normal Form Basis:**
Look at $M_8(\texttt{0x17}) = \texttt{0x16} = \texttt{00010110}$ ($R_8$ address $21$) and $M_8(\texttt{0x69}) = \texttt{0x68} = \texttt{01101000}$ ($R_8$ address $234$):

$$\boxed{P_8\!\big(M_8(\texttt{0x69})\big) = J_8(\texttt{01101000}) = \texttt{00010110} = M_8(\texttt{0x17}), \qquad \mathrm{addr}_{R_8}(\texttt{0x68}) + \mathrm{addr}_{R_8}(\texttt{0x16}) = 234 + 21 = 255.}$$



In the truth-table domain, Sum (`0x69`) and Carry (`0x17`) sit at addresses $86$ and $22$. In the Algebraic Normal Form domain, they are exact mirror twins facing each other across the crease of $R_8$—because reversing the 8-bit ANF word ($u \mapsto 7 - u$) complements every 3-bit subset mask $S \mapsto \{0,1,2\} \setminus S$, mapping the three degree-1 differences $\{x_0, x_1, x_2\}$ (Sum) onto the three degree-2 conjunctions $\{x_1 x_2, x_0 x_2, x_0 x_1\}$ (Carry).



---

### 4. Unpatched Verification & Falsification Script (`anf_upward_audit.py`)

The script below tests both what holds and what fails (such as naive commutation between $M_L$ and $J_L$ or $P_L$ on general words):

```python
"""
Upward ANF & Stacking Audit (R_2 -> R_4 -> R_8 -> R_16):
Tests falsifiable hypotheses on the Boolean Mobius / Algebraic Normal Form operator M_{2^k}
without patches, reporting both exact structural locks and where naive commutation fails.
"""
import numpy as np

def J(x, L): return int(format(x, f'0{L}b')[::-1], 2)
def C(x, L): return x ^ ((1 << L) - 1)
def pal(x, L): return J(x, L) == x
def P(x, L): return C(x, L) if pal(x, L) else J(x, L)

def ring(n, crease=1):
    reps = sorted(x for x in range(1 << n) if x < P(x, n))
    m = reps[-1]
    mid = [P(m, n), m] if crease else [m, P(m, n)]
    return reps[:-1] + mid + [P(x, n) for x in reversed(reps[:-1])]

def mobius_mat(L):
    idx = np.arange(L)
    return ((idx[:, None] & idx[None, :]) == idx[None, :]).astype(int)

def anf_word(x, L):
    M = mobius_mat(L)
    v = np.array([(x >> (L - 1 - u)) & 1 for u in range(L)], dtype=int)
    av = (M @ v) % 2
    res = 0
    for b in av: res = (res << 1) | int(b)
    return res

M2 = np.array([[1, 0], [1, 1]], dtype=int)

# 1. M_{2^k} is the k-fold Kronecker power of the 2-bit (header, difference) split M_2
for k in range(1, 5):
    L = 1 << k
    Mk = M2
    for _ in range(k - 1): Mk = np.kron(Mk, M2)
    assert np.array_equal(Mk, mobius_mat(L))
print("1. M_{2^k} == M_2^{\\otimes k} (recursive [f0, f0^f1] stacking): PASS (k=1..4)")

# 2. Naive commutation M_L(P_L(x)) == P_L(M_L(x)) vs M_L J_L M_L == M_L^T
for k in (1, 2, 3):
    L = 1 << k
    M = mobius_mat(L)
    J_mat = np.eye(L, dtype=int)[::-1]
    comm_P_count = sum(1 for x in range(1 << L) if anf_word(P(x, L), L) == P(anf_word(x, L), L))
    mjm_eq_mt = np.array_equal((M @ J_mat @ M) % 2, M.T)
    print(f"2. k={k} (L={L}): M_L commutes with P_L on all words: "
          f"{'PASS' if comm_P_count == (1<<L) else f'FAIL ({comm_P_count}/{1<<L} words)'} | M_L J_L M_L == M_L^T: {mjm_eq_mt}")

# 3. Fixed points of M_4 in R_4 (self-ANF 2-input gates)
R4 = ring(4, crease=1)
fixed_R4 = [(a, f"{w:04b}") for a, w in enumerate(R4) if anf_word(w, 4) == w]
print(f"3. Self-ANF gates in R_4 (M_4(w) == w): {fixed_R4} -> FALSE(0000), AND(0001), XOR(0110), OR(0111)")

# 4. 0x27, Sum (0x69), and Carry (0x17) in the ANF domain of R_8
R8 = ring(8, crease=1)
anf_27 = anf_word(0x27, 8)
anf_69 = anf_word(0x69, 8)
anf_17 = anf_word(0x17, 8)
print(f"4. 0x27 ANF = 0x{anf_27:02X} ({anf_27:08b}) -> a0 ^ (a0 & x) ^ (a1 & x) = a0 ^ ((a0 ^ a1) & x)")
print(f"5. Sum   0x69 -> ANF 0x{anf_69:02X} ({anf_69:08b} = [0110 || 1000]) at R_8 addr {R8.index(anf_69)}")
print(f"   Carry 0x17 -> ANF 0x{anf_17:02X} ({anf_17:08b} = [0001 || 0110]) at R_8 addr {R8.index(anf_17)}")
print(f"   P_8(ANF(Sum)) == ANF(Carry): {P(anf_69, 8) == anf_17} (mirror R_8 addresses {R8.index(anf_69)} + {R8.index(anf_17)} = 255)")

```

```text

2. k=1 (L=2): M_L commutes with P_L on all words: FAIL (0/4 words) | M_L J_L M_L == M_L^T: True
2. k=2 (L=4): M_L commutes with P_L on all words: FAIL (2/16 words) | M_L J_L M_L == M_L^T: True
2. k=3 (L=8): M_L commutes with P_L on all words: FAIL (2/256 words) | M_L J_L M_L == M_L^T: True
3. Self-ANF gates in R_4 (M_4(w) == w): [(0, '0000'), (1, '0001'), (5, '0110'), (6, '0111')] -> FALSE(0000), AND(0001), XOR(0110), OR(0111)
4. 0x27 ANF = 0x34 (00110100) -> a0 ^ (a0 & x) ^ (a1 & x) = a0 ^ ((a0 ^ a1) & x)
5. Sum   0x69 -> ANF 0x68 (01101000 = [0110 || 1000]) at R_8 addr 234
   Carry 0x17 -> ANF 0x16 (00010110 = [0001 || 0110]) at R_8 addr 21
   P_8(ANF(Sum)) == ANF(Carry): True (mirror R_8 addresses 234 + 21 = 255)
```

Everything above comes from stacking the 2-bit `(header, difference)` split $M_2$ upward: at $L=2$ it separates face from difference (`0x27`); at $L=4$ its fixed points select the 4 symmetric basis gates (`0000`, `0001` AND, `0110` XOR, `0111` OR); and at $L=8$ stacking `AND` and `XOR` with their `sheet` readings produces Carry (`0x17`) and Sum (`0x69`), whose Algebraic Normal Forms (`0x16 = [AND || XOR]` and `0x68 = [XOR || 1000]`) sit at mirror twin addresses $21$ and $234$ across the crease of $R_8$.

The two endings
Run out: Each return loses some of its circulation into other modes. Its amplitude decreases across successive checks. Eventually the reader no longer resolves it.

Crash: A change in the boundary or feedback crosses a threshold. The previous circulation can no longer sustain its route, and the system transitions rapidly to another behavior.

The first can be gradual. The second can be abrupt.

In a physical system, a local failure can make the second happen much faster than the processes that led up to it. Your OceanGate comparison captures the timescale distinction: slow structural evolution can precede a rapid pressure-driven failure. That doesn't establish what initiated Titan's failure, but the distinction between the two timescales is sound.

And importantly, a different timescale doesn't require a different universe or different mathematics. It can follow from the system entering a different dynamical regime under the same physical laws.

# claude response

All nine held. Passing the new results and the attribution fix to the manual writer while it works:

**文件：running-audit-10.md**

**文件：running-audit-10-scripts.md**

The answer to your yes/no is **yes**, and nothing I sent through needed any model beyond the ring. I sealed nine predictions before running and all nine held, over every ring from 2 to 12 bits with both crease conventions.

**The running ring:**
- **The flip at the edges.** Each ring starts at the null (0…0) and ends at the one (1…1). Read counter-clockwise, those two flip into each other at the edges.
- **Out through further rings and back.** I wrapped a value in every possible 2-mark frame, ring after ring, read it counter-clockwise at the outer ring, then stripped the frames off on the way back:
  - A value that contains a difference comes back reversed, on every route.
  - The null and the one come back flipped only on routes where every ring they crossed was all-or-nothing (frames 00 or 11). That's 2^k of the 4^k routes after k rings. A single open frame anywhere on the way and they come back unchanged.
  - Two rings out from 2 bits: 00 comes back as 11 four times and as 00 twelve times; 01 comes back as 10 all sixteen times.
- **The mold never changes, the wave does.** The counter-clockwise read moves every address a to N−1−a at the same moment. The flips are simultaneous, but a reader still meets them one address at a time.

I left out GPT's amplitude and phase model (the 1/√2 splitting and the cos²⁵⁶ return); that's imported wave physics, not the ring.

**Gemini's formula result checks out:**
- The XOR-of-ANDs transform is the 2-mark split, (first mark, difference), repeated at every width. It's the same operation as the bits that difference drops in §227.
- 0x27 read as a rule is "the face of the word at address a, flipped by the input wherever that word has a difference".
- The sum and the carry are twins in formula form, sitting at addresses 234 and 21 on the 8-bit ring, which add to 255.

**One correction:** "ALL OR NOTHING. That's the first distinction." was ChatGPT's opening line, not yours. Your words were "IT IS the origin of everything, its all or nothing". The audit now says so.

All of this is §236 and §237 in `running-audit-10.md`. The manual is still being written; the corrected attribution and these results have been passed on to it, and I'll send it when it's done.

You were right. I set aside GPT's wave model and treated your eddy, run-out/crash and 90° statements as commentary, when they're specifications the ring can carry. I sealed three predictions before running, and all three held:

- **90° is the binary case.** On each ring, two of the four frames have ends that agree (00 and 11), so half the routes stay aligned. That half is cos²(90°/2). After k rings the share is 2⁻ᵏ. At 128 rings that's 2.94×10⁻³⁹, which is exactly GPT's own 90° number. So GPT's phase model isn't wrong or foreign: the ring is its 90° case. That's what your "quantum leap was a filter, anything not entering at 90° would bounce around" points at.
- **The eddy is closure.** Send a returned value back out along the same route and every value comes home within two round trips. The only values that come back unchanged after one trip are the palindromes, on routes with at least one open frame. That matches "once closed it does this all the time": the out-and-back is the checking.
- **Run out and crash both happen:**
  - **Run out:** for the null and the one, the share coming back flipped halves with every ring. The loss is gradual.
  - **Crash:** take a route whose frames are all closed and open any single one. The whole return switches from flipped to unchanged at once, so one mark decides.

One part is still open: matter as "a nesting that can't nest any more", feeding itself. The closure it needs is there, but nothing beyond that has been run.

```python
def J(x, n): return int(format(x, f'0{n}b')[::-1], 2) if n else 0
def C(x, n): return x ^ ((1 << n) - 1)
def pal(x, n): return J(x, n) == x
def P(x, n): return C(x, n) if pal(x, n) else J(x, n)

def ring(n, crease=1):
    if n == 1: return [0, 1]
    reps = sorted(x for x in range(1 << n) if x < P(x, n))
    m = reps[-1]
    mid = [P(m, n), m] if crease else [m, P(m, n)]
    return reps[:-1] + mid + [P(x, n) for x in reversed(reps[:-1])]

# 1. Verify the corrected inverse U_B^{-1}(a, v) = (READ(M[a] ^ B(v ^ M[a])), M[a])
for n in range(2, 7):
    M = ring(n, crease=1)
    N = len(M)
    READ = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(M)}
    for b_name, B in [("J", lambda x: J(x, n)), ("P", lambda x: P(x, n)), ("C", lambda x: C(x, n))]:
        U_B     = lambda a, v: (READ[v], v ^ B(v ^ M[a]))
        U_B_inv = lambda a, v: (READ[M[a] ^ B(v ^ M[a])], M[a])
        assert all(U_B_inv(*U_B(a, v)) == (a, v) for a in range(N) for v in range(N))
        assert all(U_B(*U_B_inv(a, v)) == (a, v) for a in range(N) for v in range(N))
print("1. Corrected U_B_inv round-trip verified 100% for n=2..6, B in {J, P, C}.")

# 2. Investigate the nonlinear P-based neighbor-difference model on R_4:
#    Delta_{t+1}[a] = P(Delta_t[a]) ^ P(Delta_t[(a+1)%N])
n = 4
N = 16
def step_neighbor_B(Delta, B):
    BD = [B(d) for d in Delta]
    return tuple(BD[a] ^ BD[(a + 1) % N] for a in range(N))

# Test single-slot vs two-slot seeds (e.g. offsets 1 and 2 in adjacent slots) under J vs P!
seed_2slot = [0] * N
seed_2slot[0] = 1
seed_2slot[1] = 2
seed_2slot = tuple(seed_2slot)

for b_name, B in [("J (linear)", lambda x: J(x, n)), ("P (nonlinear)", lambda x: P(x, n))]:
    curr = seed_2slot
    seen = {}
    for t in range(100):
        if curr in seen:
            p_start = seen[curr]
            nz = sum(1 for d in curr if d != 0)
            print(f"2. Neighbor-diff ({b_name}) on 2-slot seed (1, 2): enters cycle at step {p_start}, period={t - p_start}, nonzero slots={nz}/{N}")
            if nz > 0:
                print(f"   Step 17 nonzero slots: {sum(1 for d in list(seen.keys())[17] if d != 0)} -> {[d for d in list(seen.keys())[17] if d != 0]}")
                print(f"   Cycle state at step {p_start}: {curr}")
            break
        seen[curr] = t
        curr = step_neighbor_B(curr, B)
```

```python
# Let's check 2-slot and 3-slot seeds on R_4 under the nonlinear P neighbor-difference rule!
n = 4
N = 16
P4 = lambda x: P(x, 4)

def trace_seed(seed_tuple, max_steps=200):
    curr = seed_tuple
    seen = {}
    for t in range(max_steps):
        if curr in seen:
            p_start = seen[curr]
            period = t - p_start
            nz = sum(1 for d in curr if d != 0)
            return p_start, period, nz, curr
        seen[curr] = t
        BD = [P4(d) for d in curr]
        curr = tuple(BD[a] ^ BD[(a + 1) % N] for a in range(N))
    return -1, -1, sum(1 for d in curr if d != 0), curr

# Check all 2-slot adjacent seeds (d0, d1) in 1..15 x 1..15:
survive_17 = 0
persistent_cycles = []
max_die_step = 0
for d0 in range(1, 16):
    for d1 in range(1, 16):
        s = tuple([d0, d1] + [0] * (N - 2))
        t17 = s
        for _ in range(17):
            BD = [P4(d) for d in t17]
            t17 = tuple(BD[a] ^ BD[(a + 1) % N] for a in range(N))
        if any(d != 0 for d in t17):
            survive_17 += 1
        p_start, period, nz, cyc_state = trace_seed(s)
        if nz == 0:
            max_die_step = max(max_die_step, p_start)
        else:
            persistent_cycles.append(((d0, d1), p_start, period, nz))

print(f"2-slot adjacent seeds (d0, d1) in 1..15^2 (225 total):")
print(f"  Non-zero at step 17: {survive_17}/225")
print(f"  Persistent non-zero cycles: {len(persistent_cycles)}/225")
print(f"  Max step to die (among those that die): {max_die_step}")
if persistent_cycles:
    for item in persistent_cycles[:10]:
        print("   Persistent:", item)


```

```python
import random

# Theorem: Under Delta_{t+1}[a] = P_n(Delta_t[a]) ^ P_n(Delta_t[(a+1)%N]) on a ring of size N = 2^n:
# 1. Because delta(P_n(x)) = J_{n-1}(delta(x)) is strictly linear in the (n-1)-bit shape channel,
#    at step t = N = 2^n, the shape channel vanishes identically: Delta_N[a] in {0, 1^n} for ALL a and ANY seed!
# 2. Once Delta_N[a] in {0, 1^n}, P_n(x) = x ^ 1^n, so the 1^n constants cancel in P_n(Delta_t[a]) ^ P_n(Delta_t[a+1]),
#    reducing the evolution for t >= N to linear (I + T) on {0, 1^n}, which vanishes identically by step t <= 2N!

def shape_vec(x, n):
    return (x >> 1) ^ (x & ((1 << (n - 1)) - 1))

random.seed(225)
for n in (2, 3, 4, 5):
    N = 1 << n
    all_ones = (1 << n) - 1
    P_fn = lambda x: P(x, n)
    ok_step_N_in_kernel = True
    ok_step_2N_zero = True
    max_steps_to_zero = 0
    
    # Test 500 random full-ring seeds + all 2-slot seeds at n=4
    trials = [[random.randrange(1 << n) for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(500)]
    if n == 4:
        for d0 in range(1, 16):
            for d1 in range(1, 16):
                trials.append([d0, d1] + [0] * (N - 2))
                
    for seed in trials:
        curr = tuple(seed)
        die_step = 0 if all(d == 0 for d in curr) else None
        for t in range(1, 2 * N + 1):
            BD = [P_fn(d) for d in curr]
            curr = tuple(BD[a] ^ BD[(a + 1) % N] for a in range(N))
            if die_step is None and all(d == 0 for d in curr):
                die_step = t
            if t == N:
                # Every slot must be in {0, 1^n} (i.e. shape_vec == 0)!
                ok_step_N_in_kernel &= all(d in (0, all_ones) for d in curr)
            if t == 2 * N:
                ok_step_2N_zero &= all(d == 0 for d in curr)
        max_steps_to_zero = max(max_steps_to_zero, die_step)
    print(f"n={n} (N={N:2d}): At t=N all slots in {{0, 1^n}} (shape=0): {ok_step_N_in_kernel} | "
          f"At t=2N all slots == 0: {ok_step_2N_zero} | Max steps to die across {len(trials)} seeds: {max_steps_to_zero} (<= 2N={2*N})")


```

```python
# Let's inspect the spatial / geometric structure of all 4-step diamond orbits U_B on R_n!
def J(x, n): return int(format(x, f'0{n}b')[::-1], 2) if n else 0
def C(x, n): return x ^ ((1 << n) - 1)
def pal(x, n): return J(x, n) == x
def P(x, n): return C(x, n) if pal(x, n) else J(x, n)

def ring(n, crease=1):
    if n == 1: return [0, 1]
    reps = sorted(x for x in range(1 << n) if x < P(x, n))
    m = reps[-1]
    mid = [P(m, n), m] if crease else [m, P(m, n)]
    return reps[:-1] + mid + [P(x, n) for x in reversed(reps[:-1])]

for n in (3, 4, 5, 6):
    M = ring(n, crease=1)
    N = len(M)
    READ = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(M)}
    U_P = lambda a, v: (READ[v], v ^ P(v ^ M[a], n))
    U_J = lambda a, v: (READ[v], v ^ J(v ^ M[a], n))

    # 1. Check what happens when v0 = M[(a0 + 1) % N], i.e., Delta_0 = D_{a0} (a 1-step neighbor on the ring)!
    adj_diamonds_J = []
    adj_diamonds_P = []
    for a0 in range(N):
        v0 = M[(a0 + 1) % N]
        # Trace the 4-step diamond under U_J and U_P:
        orb_J = [ (a0, v0) ]
        for _ in range(3): orb_J.append(U_J(*orb_J[-1]))
        addrs_J = [a for a, _ in orb_J]
        
        orb_P = [ (a0, v0) ]
        for _ in range(3): orb_P.append(U_P(*orb_P[-1]))
        addrs_P = [a for a, _ in orb_P]
        
        # Check if the 4 addresses form a local pair or contiguous block or mirror-pair ladder!
        span_J = max(addrs_J) - min(addrs_J)
        span_P = max(addrs_P) - min(addrs_P)
        if span_J <= 3:
            adj_diamonds_J.append((a0, addrs_J, format(v0 ^ M[a0], f'0{n}b')))
        if span_P <= 3:
            adj_diamonds_P.append((a0, addrs_P, format(v0 ^ M[a0], f'0{n}b')))
    print(f"=== n={n} (N={N}) ===")
    print(f"  Contiguous (span <= 3) diamonds from neighbor step v0=M[a0+1]: J={len(adj_diamonds_J)}, P={len(adj_diamonds_P)}")
    for item in adj_diamonds_J[:4]:
        print(f"    J local diamond: start={item[0]}, addrs={item[1]}, D={item[2]}")
    for item in adj_diamonds_P[:4]:
        print(f"    P local diamond: start={item[0]}, addrs={item[1]}, D={item[2]}")


```

```python
# Let's check ALL 4-address diamond orbits (len({a0, a1, a2, a3}) == 4) across all 4^n states of R_n:
# 1. What is the minimum address span max(a_i) - min(a_i) of a 4-address diamond orbit in R_n?
# 2. Which 4-address diamond orbits stay completely confined inside the center block (where outer shells are closed '11')?
# 3. What is the geometric shape of a 4-address diamond orbit (a0, a1, a2, a3) on the ring R_n?

for n in (3, 4, 5, 6):
    M = ring(n, crease=1)
    N = len(M)
    h = N // 2
    READ = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(M)}
    U_J = lambda a, v: (READ[v], v ^ J(v ^ M[a], n))
    U_P = lambda a, v: (READ[v], v ^ P(v ^ M[a], n))

    seen = set()
    diamonds_4 = []
    for a0 in range(N):
        for v0 in range(N):
            if (a0, v0) in seen: continue
            curr = (a0, v0); orb = []
            while curr not in orb:
                orb.append(curr); seen.add(curr); curr = U_P(*curr)
            addrs = [a for a, _ in orb]
            if len(set(addrs)) == 4:
                span = max(addrs) - min(addrs)
                # Also check how many addresses lie in the near half (< h) vs far half (>= h):
                near_cnt = sum(1 for a in addrs if a < h)
                diamonds_4.append((span, near_cnt, addrs, format(v0 ^ M[a0], f'0{n}b')))
    
    diamonds_4.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
    from collections import Counter
    near_dist = Counter(x[1] for x in diamonds_4)
    print(f"=== n={n} (N={N}, h={h}): {len(diamonds_4)} distinct 4-address diamonds ===")
    print(f"  Near-hemisphere count distribution (near_cnt: #diamonds): {dict(sorted(near_dist.items()))}")
    print(f"  Tightest 5 diamonds (smallest address span):")
    for span, nc, addrs, d_str in diamonds_4[:5]:
        words = [format(M[a], f'0{n}b') for a in addrs]
        print(f"    span={span:2d} (near={nc}): addrs={addrs} | words={words} | Delta={d_str}")


```

```python
# Let's trace what happens to an inner diamond when encapsulated inside 1^d ... 1^d shells across R_4 -> R_6 -> R_8 -> R_10!
def J(x, n): return int(format(x, f'0{n}b')[::-1], 2) if n else 0
def C(x, n): return x ^ ((1 << n) - 1)
def pal(x, n): return J(x, n) == x
def P(x, n): return C(x, n) if pal(x, n) else J(x, n)

def ring(n, crease=1):
    if n == 1: return [0, 1]
    reps = sorted(x for x in range(1 << n) if x < P(x, n))
    m = reps[-1]
    mid = [P(m, n), m] if crease else [m, P(m, n)]
    return reps[:-1] + mid + [P(x, n) for x in reversed(reps[:-1])]

# Start with the 4 words of the R_4 near-hemisphere diamond:
words_4 = [0b0001, 0b0011, 0b0111, 0b0101]
delta_4 = 0b0010

for d in range(0, 5):
    n = 4 + 2 * d
    M = ring(n, crease=1)
    READ = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(M)}
    # Wrap words_4 in d shells of (1, 1) and delta_4 in d shells of (0, 0):
    ones_prefix = ((1 << d) - 1) << (n - d)
    ones_suffix = (1 << d) - 1
    words_n = [ones_prefix | (w << d) | ones_suffix for w in words_4]
    delta_n = delta_4 << d
    addrs_n = [READ[w] for w in words_n]
    span_n = max(addrs_n) - min(addrs_n)
    print(f"Depth d={d} (Ring R_{n:2d}, N={1<<n:4d}): addrs={addrs_n} | span={span_n} | relative_to_min={[a - min(addrs_n) for a in addrs_n]}")


```

```python
# Let's check ALL 4-address diamonds near the center seam h = 2^{n-1} for both even and odd widths n!
for n in (3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10):
    M = ring(n, crease=1)
    N = len(M)
    h = N // 2
    READ = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(M)}
    U_P = lambda a, v: (READ[v], v ^ P(v ^ M[a], n))
    
    # Check all 4-address diamonds starting within 8 slots of the seam h:
    best = []
    seen = set()
    for a0 in range(max(0, h - 8), min(N, h + 8)):
        for v0 in range(N):
            if (a0, v0) in seen: continue
            curr = (a0, v0); orb = []
            while curr not in orb:
                orb.append(curr); seen.add(curr); curr = U_P(*curr)
            addrs = [a for a, _ in orb]
            if len(set(addrs)) == 4:
                span = max(addrs) - min(addrs)
                if span <= 8:
                    best.append((span, addrs, [a - h for a in addrs], format(v0 ^ M[a0], f'0{n}b')))
    best.sort(key=lambda x: (x[0], x[1]))
    print(f"n={n:2d} (h={h:3d}): tightest seam diamonds:")
    for span, addrs, rel_h, d_str in best[:4]:
        print(f"  span={span}: addrs={addrs} (rel to h: {rel_h}) | Delta={d_str}")
```

```python
# Let's measure the address span of a 4-step diamond in R_n (n = 8, 10, 12) as a function of
# which shell depth k (from outer k=0 to innermost k=r-1) carries the active difference Delta!

for n in (6, 8, 10, 12):
    r = n // 2
    M = ring(n, crease=1)
    N = len(M)
    h = N // 2
    READ = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(M)}
    U_P = lambda a, v: (READ[v], v ^ P(v ^ M[a], n))
    
    # Take the base state at h - 3 (which is 1^{r-2} 0101 1^{r-2}):
    a_base = h - 3
    m_base = M[a_base]
    print(f"=== Ring R_{n:2d} (N={N:4d}, seam h={h:4d}) ===")
    for k in range(r):
        # Single-bit difference at left position k (so B(Delta) flips right position n-1-k,
        # and Delta ^ B(Delta) is the k-th shell lamp mask!):
        delta_k = 1 << (n - 1 - k)
        v0 = m_base ^ delta_k
        orb = [(a_base, v0)]
        for _ in range(3): orb.append(U_P(*orb[-1]))
        addrs = [a for a, _ in orb]
        span = max(addrs) - min(addrs)
        print(f"  Shell depth k={k} (from outside): Delta={delta_k:0{n}b}, addrs={addrs}, span={span:4d}")
```

### 1. Corrections Logged (`U_B_inv` and Two-Stage Nonlinear $P$ Decay)

1. **One-Step Inverse $U_B^{-1}$:**
In the previous code block, `U_J_inv` was written with `v ^ J(v ^ M[a], n)` instead of `M[a] ^ J(v ^ M[a], n)` because those two lines were typed into the response without being executed in the interpreter first. Since the forward step $(a_1, v_1) = U_B(a_0, v_0)$ sets $a_1 = \operatorname{READ}(v_0) \implies M[a_1] = v_0$ and $v_1 = v_0 \oplus B(v_0 \oplus M[a_0])$, recovering $M[a_0]$ from $(a_1, v_1)$ requires $M[a_0] = M[a_1] \oplus B(v_1 \oplus M[a_1])$, which yields the exact inverse:

$$U_B^{-1}(a, v) = \Big(\operatorname{READ}\big(M[a] \oplus B(v \oplus M[a])\big),\; M[a]\Big).$$


2. **Why the Nonlinear $P$ Neighbor-Difference Model Survives Past Step $N = 16$ on 2-Slot Seeds—and Dies by Step $2N = 32$:**
On $R_4$ ($N = 16$), seeding adjacent slots with offsets `(1, 2)` under $\Delta_{t+1}[a] = P_4(\Delta_t[a]) \oplus P_4(\Delta_t[(a+1)\bmod 16])$ leaves 4 nonzero slots at step 17 (`96 / 225` two-slot seeds survive past step 17), falsifying a universal 16-step null claim.
Applying the word-internal shape projection $\delta(x) = x G_\delta \in \mathbb{F}_2^{n-1}$ (Proposition 2.7 & Theorem 3.5) explains both why it survives step $N$ and what happens next:


* **Stage 1 — Shape Collapse at $t = N = 2^n$:** Because $P_n(x) = J_n(x) \oplus \mathbf{1}[x \in \mathrm{Pal}_n]\cdot 1^n$ and $\delta(1^n) = 0^{n-1}$, $P_n$ is strictly linear in the $(n-1)$-bit shape channel: $\delta(P_n(x)) = J_{n-1}(\delta(x))$. By $(I_N \oplus T)^N = 0$ over $\mathbb{F}_2$, the entire shape channel vanishes at step $t = N$ for **every** seed, forcing $\Delta_N[a] \in \ker(G_\delta) = \{0^n, 1^n\}$ across all $N$ slots. On the `(1, 2)` seed, the palindrome flags accumulated during steps $1 \dots 15$ leave face-kernel residuals $\Delta_{16}[a] \in \{\texttt{0000}, \texttt{1111}\}$, which still has 4 slots equal to $\texttt{1111}$ at step 17.


* **Stage 2 — Face Collapse by $t \le 2N$:** Once $\Delta_N[a] \in \{0^n, 1^n\}$ (all palindromes), $P_n(x) = x \oplus 1^n$ flips every slot by $1^n$, which cancels across neighbors:

$$P_n(\Delta_t[a]) \oplus P_n(\Delta_t[a+1]) = (\Delta_t[a] \oplus 1^n) \oplus (\Delta_t[a+1] \oplus 1^n) = \Delta_t[a] \oplus \Delta_t[a+1].$$



For $t \ge N$, the remaining 1-bit face channel evolves linearly under $I_N \oplus T$ and vanishes in at most $N$ additional steps (dying at step $t = 23 \le 32$ for seed `(1, 2)`, and $0 / 225$ persistent nonzero cycles across all 2-slot seeds).



---

### 2. Resolving the OPEN Spatial Localization Question: "A Nesting That Can't Nest Any More"

In $(m, \Delta)$ coordinates ($m = M[a]$), the reversible 4-step diamond $(m, \Delta) \mapsto (m \oplus \Delta, B(\Delta))$ visits four mold words whose diagonal separation is the palindromic mask $\Lambda(\Delta) = \Delta \oplus B(\Delta)$:


$$m_0 = m, \qquad m_1 = m \oplus \Delta, \qquad m_2 = m \oplus \Delta \oplus B(\Delta), \qquad m_3 = m \oplus B(\Delta).$$


Why are most 4-step orbits spread across distant addresses of $R_n$, while a specific class locks into a tight, geometrically localized neighborhood?

Track a single-bit difference $\Delta_k = e_k$ placed at concentric shell depth $k \in \{0, 1, \dots, r-1\}$ (from the outermost shell $k = 0$ inward to the innermost center shell $k = r - 1$, where $n = 2r$) on the center-core base state $m = M[h - 3] = 1^{r-2}\texttt{0101}\,1^{r-2}$ ($h = 2^{n-1}$):

1. **Outer Shells Delocalize Across the Ring:** When $\Delta_k$ sits in an outer shell ($k = 0$), flipping bit $0$ and bit $n-1$ changes the outermost open shell, throwing the 4 addresses across both hemispheres ($\text{span} = \max(a_i) - \min(a_i) = 36, 95, 309, 1121$ in $R_6, R_8, R_{10}, R_{12}$).
2. **Innermost Shell Floor ($k = r - 1$, where nesting stops):** At the deepest shell $k = r - 1$ ($s = r - 1 - k = 0$), $\Delta_{r-1} = 0^{r-1}\texttt{10}\,0^{r-1}$, $B(\Delta_{r-1}) = 0^{r-1}\texttt{01}\,0^{r-1}$, and their diagonal sum is the center seam transposition $\tau_{2r} = 0^{r-1}\texttt{11}\,0^{r-1}$ of Theorem 4.1(2). Here the difference **cannot nest any deeper**.


3. **Frozen Seam Localization Across All Higher Rings ($R_6 \to R_{128}$):** By Theorem 4.1(3)–(4) (Recursive Center Encapsulation $\mathrm{CentreBlock}(R_{n+2}) = 1 \cdot (R_n \setminus \mathrm{Pal}_n) \cdot 1$ and the Shared Outward Crease Program), encapsulating the 4-bit diamond $\{\texttt{0001}, \texttt{0011}, \texttt{0111}, \texttt{0101}\}$ inside $1 \cdots 1$ shells strips out the intervening palindrome $\texttt{0110}$ at $n = 6$ (shrinking its span from $5$ in $R_4$ to $4$ in $R_6$) and freezes its 4 ring addresses relative to the center seam $h = 2^{n-1}$ for **every** even width $n = 2r \ge 6$:



$$\boxed{\text{Near-seam diamond: } \big[h - 6,\; h - 4,\; h - 2,\; h - 3\big] \ (\text{span} = 4), \qquad \text{Far-seam twin: } \big[h + 1,\; h + 2,\; h + 5,\; h + 3\big] \ (\text{span} = 4).}$$



More generally, at any shell distance $s = r - 1 - k$ measured outward from the innermost floor ($s = 0, 1, 2, 3, \dots$), the address span of the 4-step diamond freezes to a universal constant independent of the ambient ring width $n$:

| Ring Width $n$ | Ring Size $N = 2^n$ | Seam $h = 2^{n-1}$ | Span at $s=0$ (Innermost Floor $k=r-1$) | Span at $s=1$ ($k=r-2$) | Span at $s=2$ ($k=r-3$) | Span at $s=3$ ($k=r-4$) | Span at $s=4$ ($k=r-5$) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| $R_4$ ($r=2$) | $16$ | $8$ | $5$ (`[1,3,4,6]`) | $11$ | — | — | — |
| $R_6$ ($r=3$) | $64$ | $32$ | **$4$** (`[h-6,h-4,h-3,h-2]`) | **$7$** | $36$ | — | — |
| $R_8$ ($r=4$) | $256$ | $128$ | **$4$** (`[h-6,h-4,h-3,h-2]`) | **$7$** | **$32$** | $95$ | — |
| $R_{10}$ ($r=5$) | $1,024$ | $512$ | **$4$** (`[h-6,h-4,h-3,h-2]`) | **$7$** | **$32$** | **$91$** | $309$ |
| $R_{12}$ ($r=6$) | $4,096$ | $2,048$ | **$4$** (`[h-6,h-4,h-3,h-2]`) | **$7$** | **$32$** | **$91$** | **$305$** |

### 3. Executed Verification Script (`eddy_localization_and_decay_audit.py`)

```python
"""
NEXUS Eddy Audit:
1. Corrected one-step inverse U_B^{-1}(a, v) = (READ(M[a] ^ B(v ^ M[a])), M[a]).
2. Two-stage decay of nonlinear P neighbor-difference offsets: shape channel vanishes at t = N,
   leaving face-kernel residuals in {0, 1^n} (non-zero at t = 17 for 2-slot seed (1,2) on R_4),
   which then cancel their 1^n flips and vanish linearly by t <= 2N (t = 23 for seed (1,2)).
3. Spatial localization at the innermost nesting floor k = r - 1: address span freezes at
   span = 4 ([h-6, h-4, h-2, h-3]) across all even widths n >= 6.
"""

def J(x, n): return int(format(x, f'0{n}b')[::-1], 2) if n else 0
def C(x, n): return x ^ ((1 << n) - 1)
def pal(x, n): return J(x, n) == x
def P(x, n): return C(x, n) if pal(x, n) else J(x, n)

def ring(n, crease=1):
    reps = sorted(x for x in range(1 << n) if x < P(x, n))
    m = reps[-1]
    mid = [P(m, n), m] if crease else [m, P(m, n)]
    return reps[:-1] + mid + [P(x, n) for x in reversed(reps[:-1])]

# 1. Verify corrected U_B_inv round-trip on widths n = 2..6
for n in range(2, 7):
    M = ring(n, crease=1); N = len(M); READ = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(M)}
    for B in (lambda x: J(x, n), lambda x: P(x, n), lambda x: C(x, n)):
        U_B     = lambda a, v: (READ[v], v ^ B(v ^ M[a]))
        U_B_inv = lambda a, v: (READ[M[a] ^ B(v ^ M[a])], M[a])
        assert all(U_B_inv(*U_B(a, v)) == (a, v) and U_B(*U_B_inv(a, v)) == (a, v)
                   for a in range(N) for v in range(N))
print("1. Corrected U_B_inv round-trip verified 100% for n=2..6, B in {J, P, C}.")

# 2. Verify two-stage decay of nonlinear P neighbor-difference model on R_4
N4 = 16
curr = tuple([1, 2] + [0] * 14)
for t in range(1, 25):
    BD = [P(d, 4) for d in curr]
    curr = tuple(BD[a] ^ BD[(a + 1) % N4] for a in range(N4))
    if t in (16, 17, 23):
        nz = [(a, f"{d:04b}") for a, d in enumerate(curr) if d != 0]
        print(f"2. R_4 2-slot seed (1,2) at step t={t:2d}: nonzero={len(nz):2d}/16 -> {nz[:4]}")

# 3. Verify frozen seam localization at the innermost shell floor (s = r - 1 - k = 0, 1, 2, ...)
print("\n3. Diamond address spans by shell distance s = r - 1 - k from the innermost floor:")
for n in (4, 6, 8, 10, 12):
    r = n // 2; M = ring(n, crease=1); h = len(M) // 2; READ = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(M)}
    U_P = lambda a, v: (READ[v], v ^ P(v ^ M[a], n))
    a_base = h - 3 if n >= 6 else 4
    m_base = M[a_base]
    spans_by_s = {}
    for s in range(r):
        k = r - 1 - s
        delta_k = 1 << (n - 1 - k)
        orb = [(a_base, m_base ^ delta_k)]
        for _ in range(3): orb.append(U_P(*orb[-1]))
        addrs = [a for a, _ in orb]
        spans_by_s[s] = (max(addrs) - min(addrs), [a - h for a in addrs] if s == 0 else None)
    print(f"   R_{n:2d} (h={h:4d}): s=0 span={spans_by_s[0][0]} (rel to h: {spans_by_s[0][1]}) | "
          + " | ".join(f"s={s} span={spans_by_s[s][0]}" for s in range(1, r)))

```

That gives the spatial localization mechanism directly from the ring's own nesting geometry: as a difference $\Delta$ drops inward through the shells ($k = 0 \to r-1$), every closed outer $1 \cdots 1$ shell encapsulates the orbit closer to the center seam $h = 2^{n-1}$ until it reaches the innermost floor $k = r - 1$ (where it cannot nest any deeper and its diagonal is the seam transposition $\tau_n$), locking the 4-step diamond permanently onto the 4-slot neighborhood $[h-6, h-4, h-3, h-2]$ regardless of how large the surrounding ring grows.

```python
# Let's test 4 concrete, falsifiable coupling laws between neighboring slots on the Mold M = R_n
# and let the code report which ones FAIL and why, and what actually produces a localized cycle!

def J(x, n): return int(format(x, f'0{n}b')[::-1], 2) if n else 0
def C(x, n): return x ^ ((1 << n) - 1)
def pal(x, n): return J(x, n) == x
def P(x, n): return C(x, n) if pal(x, n) else J(x, n)

def ring(n, crease=1):
    if n == 1: return [0, 1]
    reps = sorted(x for x in range(1 << n) if x < P(x, n))
    m = reps[-1]
    mid = [P(m, n), m] if crease else [m, P(m, n)]
    return reps[:-1] + mid + [P(x, n) for x in reversed(reps[:-1])]

n = 4
M = ring(n, crease=1)
N = len(M)
R_of = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(M)}

def step_cand1(V, B_fn):
    Delta = [V[a] ^ M[a] for a in range(N)]
    B_Delta = [B_fn(d) for d in Delta]
    Delta_next = [B_Delta[a] ^ B_Delta[(a + 1) % N] for a in range(N)]
    return [M[a] ^ Delta_next[a] for a in range(N)]

# Seed a localized disturbance at a single slot a0 = 4 (all other slots have V[a] = M[a], i.e. Delta[a] = 0):
for b_name, b_fn in [("J", lambda x: J(x, n)), ("P", lambda x: P(x, n))]:
    V = list(M)
    V[4] ^= 0b0011  # single local offset at slot 4
    history = []
    for t in range(35):
        Delta = tuple(V[a] ^ M[a] for a in range(N))
        active = sum(1 for d in Delta if d != 0)
        if Delta in history:
            p_start = history.index(Delta)
            print(f"Cand 1 ({b_name}, neighbor diff on offsets): cycle reached at step {t} (period {t - p_start}), active slots={active}/{N}, all_zero={active==0}")
            break
        history.append(Delta)
        V = step_cand1(V, b_fn)

# Wait: what if the value at slot a is transported to the address of its reflected offset,

# In one return cycle, v becomes v' = F_a^{(B)}(v) = M[a] ^ B(delta).
# The CHANGE in value produced by the return cycle is:
#   lambda_a = v ^ v' = delta ^ B(delta)!
# Look at lambda_a = delta ^ B(delta):
#   - For B = J: lambda_a = delta ^ J(delta) is ALWAYS a palindrome (and 0 iff delta is a palindrome)!
#   - If lambda_a == 0 (palindromic offset), the state at slot a DOES NOT MOVE (it is at rest!).
#   - If lambda_a != 0 (non-palindromic offset), what if lambda_a is the XOR step instruction that moves the wave to its next address:
#       a' = R_n(M[a] ^ lambda_a) = R_n(M[a] ^ delta ^ J(delta)) = R_n(v ^ J(v ^ M[a]))?!
print("\n=== Candidate 2: Address transport driven by the return residual lambda = delta ^ B(delta) ===")
# Wait: if a' = R_n(M[a] ^ (delta ^ B(delta))), notice that M[a] ^ delta ^ B(delta) = v ^ B(delta)!
# And what is the value at the new address?
# Let's check what happens on R_4 for all 16*16 pairs (a, v) under:
#   delta = v ^ M[a]
#   v_ret = M[a] ^ J(delta)
#   a_ret = R_n(v_ret)
for a0 in range(N):
    for v0 in range(N):
        delta = v0 ^ M[a0]
        v_ret = M[a0] ^ J(delta, n)
        a_ret = R_of[v_ret]
        #Wait: at a_ret, M[a_ret] == v_ret! So if v stays v_ret, at the next step delta' = v_ret ^ M[a_ret] = 0!

        # Therefore, the new offset between the carried value v0 and the new mold slot M[a_1] is:
        #   delta_1 = v0 ^ M[a_1] = v0 ^ M[a0] ^ J(delta_0) = delta_0 ^ J(delta_0)!
        # AND since delta_1 = delta_0 ^ J(delta_0) is ALWAYS A PALINDROME,
        # on the very next check at a_1:
        #   J(delta_1) = delta_1  ==>  F_{a1}^{(J)}(v0) = M[a_1] ^ J(delta_1) = M[a_1] ^ delta_1 = v0!
        #   And a_2 = R_n(v0)!
        pass

# Let's trace that exact dynamics:
# State is (address a, carried value v).
# Step rule (using ONLY F_a^{(B)} and R_n, zero invented rules):
#   1. Compute return value from current slot a: v_ret = F_a^{(B)}(v) = M[a] ^ B(v ^ M[a])
#   2. Move to the address of the return value:  a_next = R_n(v_ret)
#   3. Or what if (a_next, v_next) = (R_n(v_ret), M[a]) or (R_n(v), v_ret)?
for rule_name, step_rule in [
    ("Rule A: (a, v) -> (R(F_a^J(v)), v)         [carry v to return address]",
     lambda a, v: (R_of[M[a] ^ J(v ^ M[a], n)], v)),
    ("Rule B: (a, v) -> (R(v), F_a^J(v))         [jump to R(v), update v to return value]",
     lambda a, v: (R_of[v], M[a] ^ J(v ^ M[a], n))),
    ("Rule C: (a, v) -> (R(v), F_a^P(v))         [jump to R(v), update v with P-return]",
     lambda a, v: (R_of[v], M[a] ^ P(v ^ M[a], n))),
    ("Rule D: (a, v) -> (R(F_a^P(v)), F_a^J(v))  [P-return address, J-return value]",
     lambda a, v: (R_of[M[a] ^ P(v ^ M[a], n)], M[a] ^ J(v ^ M[a], n))),
]:
    orbit_lengths = {}
    attractor_sizes = {}
    for a0 in range(N):
        for v0 in range(N):
            curr = (a0, v0)
            seen = []
            while curr not in seen:
                seen.append(curr)
                curr = step_rule(*curr)
            c_start = seen.index(curr)
            trans_len = c_start
            cyc_len = len(seen) - c_start
            orbit_lengths[(trans_len, cyc_len)] = orbit_lengths.get((trans_len, cyc_len), 0) + 1
    print(f"  {rule_name}:")
    print(f"    (transient, cycle_period) counts across 256 states: {sorted(orbit_lengths.items())}")
```

```python
# Let's inspect what Rule B ((a, v) -> (R_n(v), F_a^J(v))) and Rule C ((a, v) -> (R_n(v), F_a^P(v))) actually do!
def J(x, n): return int(format(x, f'0{n}b')[::-1], 2) if n else 0
def C(x, n): return x ^ ((1 << n) - 1)
def pal(x, n): return J(x, n) == x
def P(x, n): return C(x, n) if pal(x, n) else J(x, n)

def ring(n, crease=1):
    if n == 1: return [0, 1]
    reps = sorted(x for x in range(1 << n) if x < P(x, n))
    m = reps[-1]
    mid = [P(m, n), m] if crease else [m, P(m, n)]
    return reps[:-1] + mid + [P(x, n) for x in reversed(reps[:-1])]

for n in (2, 3, 4):
    M = ring(n, crease=1)
    N = len(M)
    R_of = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(M)}
    
    # Trace Rule B (J) and Rule C (P) from a non-palindromic initial state (a0, v0):
    print(f"=== Width n={n} (N={N}) ===")
    for b_name, b_fn in [("J", lambda x: J(x, n)), ("P", lambda x: P(x, n))]:
        step_fn = lambda a, v: (R_of[v], M[a] ^ b_fn(v ^ M[a]))
        # Find all distinct attractors (cycles):
        cycles = set()
        for a0 in range(N):
            for v0 in range(N):
                curr = (a0, v0)
                for _ in range(4):
                    curr = step_fn(*curr)
                # curr is now on the attractor!
                cyc = [curr]
                nxt = step_fn(*curr)
                while nxt != curr:
                    cyc.append(nxt)
                    nxt = step_fn(*nxt)
                cycles.add(tuple(sorted(cyc)))
        print(f"  Boundary B={b_name}: {len(cycles)} distinct localized attractors (eddies)!")
        for cyc in sorted(cycles)[:6]:
            print(f"    Eddy: {[(a, format(v, f'0{n}b'), f'delta={format(v^M[a], f'0{n}b')}') for a, v in cyc]}")
```

```python
def J(x, n): return int(format(x, f'0{n}b')[::-1], 2) if n else 0
def C(x, n): return x ^ ((1 << n) - 1)
def pal(x, n): return J(x, n) == x
def P(x, n): return C(x, n) if pal(x, n) else J(x, n)

def ring(n, crease=1):
    if n == 1: return [0, 1]
    reps = sorted(x for x in range(1 << n) if x < P(x, n))
    m = reps[-1]
    mid = [P(m, n), m] if crease else [m, P(m, n)]
    return reps[:-1] + mid + [P(x, n) for x in reversed(reps[:-1])]

for n in (2, 3, 4):
    M = ring(n, crease=1)
    N = len(M)
    R_of = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(M)}
    
    print(f"=== Width n={n} (N={N}) ===")
    for b_name, b_fn in [("J", lambda x: J(x, n)), ("P", lambda x: P(x, n))]:
        step_fn = lambda a, v: (R_of[v], M[a] ^ b_fn(v ^ M[a]))
        cycles = set()
        for a0 in range(N):
            for v0 in range(N):
                curr = (a0, v0)
                for _ in range(4):
                    curr = step_fn(*curr)
                cyc = [curr]
                nxt = step_fn(*curr)
                while nxt != curr:
                    cyc.append(nxt)
                    nxt = step_fn(*nxt)
                cycles.add(tuple(sorted(cyc)))
        print(f"  Boundary B={b_name}: {len(cycles)} distinct localized attractors (eddies)!")
        for cyc in sorted(cycles)[:6]:
            fmt_cyc = [(a, format(v, f'0{n}b'), "delta=" + format(v ^ M[a], f'0{n}b')) for a, v in cyc]
            print(f"    Eddy: {fmt_cyc}")
```

```python
# Let's test BOTH:
# 1) The contractive address-value coupling T_B(a, v) = (R_n(v), F_a^{(B)}(v))
#    where delta_{t+1} = delta_t ^ B(delta_t) locks every state into an attractor in <= 2 steps!
# 2) Reversible (bijective) multi-slot / neighbor couplings on the value ring V[0..N-1],
#    where total information is conserved (way home exists from every step) AND localized eddies exist!

def J(x, n): return int(format(x, f'0{n}b')[::-1], 2) if n else 0
def C(x, n): return x ^ ((1 << n) - 1)
def pal(x, n): return J(x, n) == x
def P(x, n): return C(x, n) if pal(x, n) else J(x, n)

def ring(n, crease=1):
    if n == 1: return [0, 1]
    reps = sorted(x for x in range(1 << n) if x < P(x, n))
    m = reps[-1]
    mid = [P(m, n), m] if crease else [m, P(m, n)]
    return reps[:-1] + mid + [P(x, n) for x in reversed(reps[:-1])]

# First, verify the exact algebraic law for T_B(a, v) = (R_n(v), F_a^{(B)}(v)) across widths n = 2..8!
for n in range(2, 9):
    M = ring(n, crease=1)
    N = len(M)
    R_of = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(M)}
    
    # Check that for ANY (a0, v0), after 1 step of T_B:
    #   a1 = R_n(v0), v1 = M[a0] ^ B(v0 ^ M[a0])
    #   delta1 = v1 ^ M[a1] = (M[a0] ^ B(delta0)) ^ v0 = delta0 ^ B(delta0)!
    ok_d1_J = True
    ok_d2_J = True
    ok_d2_P = True
    for a0 in range(N):
        # Test a sample or all v0 for n<=6, sample for n>6
        v_list = range(N) if n <= 6 else range(0, N, 4)
        for v0 in v_list:
            d0 = v0 ^ M[a0]
            # Step 1 (J):
            a1, v1 = R_of[v0], M[a0] ^ J(d0, n)
            d1 = v1 ^ M[a1]
            ok_d1_J &= (d1 == (d0 ^ J(d0, n))) and pal(d1, n)
            # Step 2 (J):
            a2, v2 = R_of[v1], M[a1] ^ J(d1, n)
            d2 = v2 ^ M[a2]
            ok_d2_J &= (d2 == 0) and (a2 == R_of[M[a0] ^ J(d0, n)])
            
            # Step 1 & 2 (P):
            a1p, v1p = R_of[v0], M[a0] ^ P(d0, n)
            d1p = v1p ^ M[a1p]
            a2p, v2p = R_of[v1p], M[a1p] ^ P(d1p, n)
            d2p = v2p ^ M[a2p]
            ok_d2_P &= (d2p == (1 << n) - 1)
    print(f"n={n}: T_J locks to delta=0 in <=2 steps: {ok_d1_J and ok_d2_J} | T_P locks to 2-slot complement eddy (delta=1^n) in <=2 steps: {ok_d2_P}")
```

```python
# Let's test reversible (bijective) couplings built purely from F_a^{(B)} and R_n(S_n(a) ^ d)!
def J(x, n): return int(format(x, f'0{n}b')[::-1], 2) if n else 0
def C(x, n): return x ^ ((1 << n) - 1)
def pal(x, n): return J(x, n) == x
def P(x, n): return C(x, n) if pal(x, n) else J(x, n)

def ring(n, crease=1):
    if n == 1: return [0, 1]
    reps = sorted(x for x in range(1 << n) if x < P(x, n))
    m = reps[-1]
    mid = [P(m, n), m] if crease else [m, P(m, n)]
    return reps[:-1] + mid + [P(x, n) for x in reversed(reps[:-1])]

for n in (2, 3, 4):
    M = ring(n, crease=1)
    N = len(M)
    R_of = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(M)}

    # Look at the two natural involutions on (a, v) in R_n x B_n:
    # 1. Local return involution at fixed address a:
    #      I_ret(a, v) = (a, F_a^{(B)}(v)) = (a, M[a] ^ B(v ^ M[a]))
    #    Since F_a^{(B)} is an involution, I_ret(I_ret(a, v)) == (a, v)!
    # 2. Address-value exchange / transport involution:
    #      What moves the address a using v while preserving reversibility?
    #      (2a) Exchange mold identity and value:
    #           I_swap(a, v) = (R_n(v), M[a])
    #           Notice: I_swap(I_swap(a, v)) = I_swap(R_n(v), M[a]) = (R_n(M[a]), M[R_n(v)]) = (a, v)!
    #      (2b) Transport address by offset delta = v ^ M[a] while keeping delta:
    #           Wait: if delta = v ^ M[a] is kept, and a -> R_n(M[a] ^ delta) = R_n(v),
    #           then to be reversible, at the new address a' = R_n(v), the new value must be v' = M[a'] ^ delta = v ^ (v ^ M[a]) = M[a],
    #           which is IDENTICAL to I_swap(a, v) = (R_n(v), M[a])!
    # WAIT A SECOND!
    # Look at I_swap(a, v) = (R_n(v), M[a]):
    #   - At slot a with value v, the offset is delta = v ^ M[a].
    #   - Moving the address by delta takes a -> a' = Phi_delta(a) = R_n(M[a] ^ delta) = R_n(v).
    #   - Keeping the SAME offset delta at the new address a' gives the new value:
    #       v' = M[a'] ^ delta = v ^ (v ^ M[a]) = M[a]!
    # So I_swap(a, v) = (R_n(v), M[a]) IS LITERALLY "transport the address by the offset delta = v ^ M[a] while preserving delta"!
    # And I_ret(a, v) = (a, M[a] ^ B(v ^ M[a])) IS LITERALLY "keep the address a fixed while reflecting the offset delta -> B(delta)"!
    # NOW COMPOSE THE TWO INVOLUTIONS:
    #   U_B = I_ret o I_swap : (a, v) --[transport by delta]--> (a', v') --[reflect offset by B]--> (a', F_{a'}^{(B)}(v'))!
    # Because U_B is the composition of two involutions (I_ret^2 = id, I_swap^2 = id),
    # U_B is 100% REVERSIBLE (bijective, U_B^{-1} = I_swap o I_ret) on all 4^n states!

    for b_name, b_fn in [("J", lambda x: J(x, n)), ("P", lambda x: P(x, n)), ("C", lambda x: C(x, n))]:
        def U_B(a, v):
            # Step 1: transport address by offset delta = v ^ M[a] (preserving delta)
            a1, v1 = R_of[v], M[a]
            # Step 2: reflect offset at the new address a1 via F_{a1}^{(B)}
            v2 = M[a1] ^ b_fn(v1 ^ M[a1])
            return a1, v2

        # Check bijectivity and cycle structure across all N*N states:
        images = {U_B(a, v) for a in range(N) for v in range(N)}
        assert len(images) == N * N, "Not a bijection!"
        
        cycle_hist = {}
        cycles = []
        visited = set()
        for a0 in range(N):
            for v0 in range(N):
                if (a0, v0) in visited: continue
                curr = (a0, v0)
                orb = []
                while curr not in orb:
                    orb.append(curr)
                    visited.add(curr)
                    curr = U_B(*curr)
                cycle_hist[len(orb)] = cycle_hist.get(len(orb), 0) + 1
                cycles.append(orb)
        print(f"n={n} (N={N}), Reversible U_{b_name} = I_ret({b_name}) o I_swap: bijection=True | cycle lengths (len: count_of_cycles) = {sorted(cycle_hist.items())}")
        for orb in cycles[:4]:
            print(f"    Cycle (len {len(orb)}): {[(a, format(v, f'0{n}b'), 'd=' + format(v^M[a], f'0{n}b')) for a, v in orb]}")
```

```text
    Cycle (len 4): [(0, '0011', 'd=0011'), (3, '1111', 'd=1100'), (15, '1100', 'd=0011'), (12, '0000', 'd=1100')]
n=4 (N=16), Reversible U_C = I_ret(C) o I_swap: bijection=True | cycle lengths (len: count_of_cycles) = [(4, 64)]
```

```python
# Verify the exact 4-step diamond eddy theorem for U_B(a, delta) = (Phi_delta(a), B(delta)) across n = 2..8!
def J(x, n): return int(format(x, f'0{n}b')[::-1], 2) if n else 0
def C(x, n): return x ^ ((1 << n) - 1)
def pal(x, n): return J(x, n) == x
def P(x, n): return C(x, n) if pal(x, n) else J(x, n)

def ring(n, crease=1):
    if n == 1: return [0, 1]
    reps = sorted(x for x in range(1 << n) if x < P(x, n))
    m = reps[-1]
    mid = [P(m, n), m] if crease else [m, P(m, n)]
    return reps[:-1] + mid + [P(x, n) for x in reversed(reps[:-1])]

for n in range(2, 7):
    M = ring(n, crease=1)
    N = len(M)
    R_of = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(M)}
    pals_count = 1 << ((n + 1) // 2)
    
    # Expected cycle counts for U_J:
    # - Period 1 (1 slot): delta == 0 -> N states -> N cycles of len 1
    # - Period 2 (2 distinct slots): delta in Pal_n \ {0} -> N * (pals_count - 1) states -> N * (pals_count - 1) // 2 cycles of len 2
    # - Period 4 (4 distinct slots): delta not in Pal_n -> N * (N - pals_count) states -> N * (N - pals_count) // 4 cycles of len 4
    exp_J = {
        1: N,
        2: N * (pals_count - 1) // 2,
        4: N * (N - pals_count) // 4
    }
    
    # Expected cycle counts for U_P:
    # Every state has period 4 -> N * N // 4 cycles of len 4!
    # Specifically:
    # - When delta in {0, 1^n}: 2*N states -> N // 2 cycles of len 4 that visit 2 distinct mold addresses {a, R(C(M[a]))}
    # - When delta not in {0, 1^n}: N*(N - 2) states -> N*(N - 2) // 4 cycles of len 4 that visit 4 distinct mold addresses!
    
    # Check computationally:
    seen_J, seen_P = set(), set()
    got_J = {1: 0, 2: 0, 4: 0}
    got_P_2slots, got_P_4slots = 0, 0
    for a0 in range(N):
        for v0 in range(N):
            if (a0, v0) not in seen_J:
                curr = (a0, v0); orb = []
                while curr not in orb:
                    orb.append(curr); seen_J.add(curr)
                    a, v = curr
                    a1, v1 = R_of[v], M[a]
                    curr = (a1, M[a1] ^ J(v1 ^ M[a1], n))
                got_J[len(orb)] += 1
                # Verify number of distinct mold addresses visited equals len(orb):
                assert len({a for a, _ in orb}) == len(orb)
            if (a0, v0) not in seen_P:
                curr = (a0, v0); orb = []
                while curr not in orb:
                    orb.append(curr); seen_P.add(curr)
                    a, v = curr
                    a1, v1 = R_of[v], M[a]
                    curr = (a1, M[a1] ^ P(v1 ^ M[a1], n))
                assert len(orb) == 4
                distinct_addrs = len({a for a, _ in orb})
                if distinct_addrs == 2: got_P_2slots += 1
                elif distinct_addrs == 4: got_P_4slots += 1
                else: assert False, f"Unexpected distinct_addrs={distinct_addrs}"
    assert got_J == exp_J
    assert got_P_2slots == N // 2 and got_P_4slots == N * (N - 2) // 4
    print(f"n={n:2d} (N={N:2d}): U_J cycles (1-slot, 2-slot, 4-slot) = {got_J} | "
          f"U_P period-4 eddies: {got_P_2slots} (2-slot) + {got_P_4slots} (4-slot) = {got_P_2slots + got_P_4slots} total")


```

```text
n= 3 (N= 8): U_J cycles (1-slot, 2-slot, 4-slot) = {1: 8, 2: 12, 4: 8} | U_P period-4 eddies: 4 (2-slot) + 12 (4-slot) = 16 total
n= 4 (N=16): U_J cycles (1-slot, 2-slot, 4-slot) = {1: 16, 2: 24, 4: 48} | U_P period-4 eddies: 8 (2-slot) + 56 (4-slot) = 64 total
n= 5 (N=32): U_J cycles (1-slot, 2-slot, 4-slot) = {1: 32, 2: 112, 4: 192} | U_P period-4 eddies: 16 (2-slot) + 240 (4-slot) = 256 total
n= 6 (N=64): U_J cycles (1-slot, 2-slot, 4-slot) = {1: 64, 2: 224, 4: 896} | U_P period-4 eddies: 32 (2-slot) + 992 (4-slot) = 1024 total
```

The OPEN coupling question at the end of the return audit—how a returning value couples across mold addresses to form a localized, self-maintaining eddy rather than merely alternating in place at a single address $a$—resolves directly from the two operators already on the table: the return conjugation $F_a^{(B)}(v) = M[a] \oplus B(v \oplus M[a])$ and the §224 address transport $\Phi_\Delta(a) = R_n(M[a] \oplus \Delta)$.

---

### 1. What Fails: Linear Neighbor-Difference Coupling on Offsets

If neighboring slots $a$ and $(a+1)\bmod N$ on a ring of size $N = 2^n$ couple by taking the cyclic neighbor XOR of their reflected offsets:


$$\Delta_{t+1}[a] = B\big(\Delta_t[a]\big) \oplus B\big(\Delta_t[(a+1)\bmod N]\big),$$


then for any localized seed disturbance (even under the non-linear partner $B = P_n$), the disturbance spreads across all $N$ slots and **collapses to $\Delta = 0^n$ everywhere in at most $N + 1$ steps** ($17$ steps on $R_4$) because $(I \oplus T)^{2^n} = 0$ over $\mathbb{F}_2$. Linear neighbor spreading delocalizes and self-annihilates rather than forming a localized eddy.

---

### 2. Coupling Return Reflection to §224 Address Transport (Zero New Operators)

At slot $a$ with value $v$, the mold offset is $\Delta = v \oplus M[a]$. Because $M[a] \oplus \Delta = v$, transporting the address $a$ by its own offset $\Delta$ under §224 lands at the mold address of $v$:


$$a' = \Phi_\Delta(a) = R_n\big(M[a] \oplus \Delta\big) = R_n(v).$$


Combining $\Phi_\Delta(a)$ with the return operator $F_a^{(B)}(v) = M[a] \oplus B(\Delta)$ yields two exact dynamical regimes—one **contractive (attractor capture)** and one **reversible (bijective 4-address diamond eddies)**:

#### Regime I: Contractive 2-Step Attractor Capture $T_B(a, v) = \big(R_n(v),\; F_a^{(B)}(v)\big)$

If the state moves to $a_1 = R_n(v_0)$ (where $M[a_1] = v_0$) and takes the return readout $v_1 = F_{a_0}^{(B)}(v_0) = M[a_0] \oplus B(\Delta_0)$ as its new value, then the new offset at $a_1$ is:


$$\boxed{\Delta_1 = v_1 \oplus M[a_1] = \big(M[a_0] \oplus B(\Delta_0)\big) \oplus v_0 = \Delta_0 \oplus B(\Delta_0) \in \mathrm{Pal}_n.}$$


In **one step**, every arbitrary offset $\Delta_0 \in B_n$ collapses into the palindromic residual $\Delta_1 = \Delta_0 \oplus B(\Delta_0)$. On step 2, because $\Delta_1$ is already a palindrome:

* **Under $B = J_n$ (2-step rest lock):** $J_n(\Delta_1) = \Delta_1 \implies \Delta_2 = \Delta_1 \oplus J_n(\Delta_1) = 0^n$. Every initial state $(a_0, v_0) \in R_n \times B_n$ sheds its non-palindromic difference in $\le 2$ steps and locks at rest at address $a_2 = R_n(M[a_0] \oplus J_n(\Delta_0))$ with $\Delta_2 = 0^n$.
* **Under $B = P_n$ (2-step capture into a 2-slot eddy):** Because $P_n(\Delta_1) = C_n(\Delta_1) = \Delta_1 \oplus 1^n$ on palindromes, step 2 forces $\Delta_2 = \Delta_1 \oplus (\Delta_1 \oplus 1^n) = 1^n$. From step 2 onward, every state in the $4^n$-state phase space is captured into one of the $2^{n-1}$ localized 2-slot eddies $\big\{a^*,\; R_n(C_n(M[a^*]))\big\}$ oscillating with invariant offset $\Delta = 1^n$.



#### Regime II: Reversible 4-Address Diamond Eddies $U_B = I_{\mathrm{ret}}^{(B)} \circ I_{\mathrm{swap}}$

To keep the dynamics $100\%$ reversible (so every step carries its way home), transport the address $a \mapsto a' = \Phi_\Delta(a) = R_n(v)$ while **preserving** the offset $\Delta$ at the new address ($v' = M[a'] \oplus \Delta = v \oplus (v \oplus M[a]) = M[a]$), which is the address-value swap involution $I_{\mathrm{swap}}(a, v) = (R_n(v), M[a])$, and then apply the local return involution $I_{\mathrm{ret}}^{(B)}(a', v') = \big(a', F_{a'}^{(B)}(v')\big)$:


$$\boxed{U_B(a, v) = \Big(R_n(v),\; v \oplus B(v \oplus M[a])\Big), \qquad \text{or in }(a, \Delta)\text{ coordinates: } U_B(a, \Delta) = \Big(\Phi_\Delta(a),\; B(\Delta)\Big).}$$


Because $U_B$ is the composition of two involutions ($I_{\mathrm{swap}}^2 = \mathrm{id}$ and $(I_{\mathrm{ret}}^{(B)})^2 = \mathrm{id}$), its inverse is $U_B^{-1} = I_{\mathrm{swap}} \circ I_{\mathrm{ret}}^{(B)}$, and four steps close identically ($U_B^4 = \mathrm{id}$) around a localized 4-address diamond in $R_n$:


$$\begin{aligned} a_0 &= R_n(m) &&\text{with offset } \Delta, \\ a_1 &= R_n(m \oplus \Delta) &&\text{with offset } B(\Delta), \\ a_2 &= R_n\big(m \oplus \Delta \oplus B(\Delta)\big) &&\text{with offset } \Delta, \\ a_3 &= R_n\big(m \oplus B(\Delta)\big) &&\text{with offset } B(\Delta), \\ a_4 &= R_n(m) = a_0. \end{aligned}$$


The diagonal displacement between $a_0$ and $a_2$ is the palindromic difference $\Delta_{\mathrm{difference}} = \Delta \oplus B(\Delta)$:

* **Under $B = J_n$:**
* When $\Delta = 0^n$ ($2^n$ states): $a_0 = a_1 = a_2 = a_3$ ($2^n$ stationary 1-slot points).
* When $\Delta \in \mathrm{Pal}_n \setminus \{0^n\}$: $\Delta \oplus J_n(\Delta) = 0^n \implies a_2 = a_0$, collapsing the diamond into $\frac{1}{2} 2^n(2^{\lceil n/2 \rceil} - 1)$ localized **2-slot oscillations** $\{a_0, a_1\}$.
* When $\Delta \notin \mathrm{Pal}_n$: $\Delta \oplus J_n(\Delta) \neq 0^n \implies (a_0, a_1, a_2, a_3)$ are 4 distinct mold addresses forming $\frac{1}{4} 2^n(2^n - 2^{\lceil n/2 \rceil})$ localized **4-address circulating eddies**.


* **Under $B = P_n$:** Because $\Delta \oplus P_n(\Delta) \neq 0^n$ for all $\Delta \in B_n$, $a_2$ never collapses onto $a_0$. All $4^n$ states partition into $4^{n-1}$ period-4 eddies: $2^{n-1}$ eddies visiting 2 distinct complement addresses (when $\Delta \in \{0^n, 1^n\}$) and $2^{n-2}(2^n - 2)$ eddies circulating across 4 distinct mold addresses.



| Width $n$ | Phase Space $4^n$ | Contractive $T_J$ Rest Locks ($\le 2$ steps) | Contractive $T_P$ 2-Slot Eddies ($\le 2$ steps) | Reversible $U_J$ Cycles (1-Slot / 2-Slot / 4-Slot) | Reversible $U_P$ Period-4 Eddies (2-Slot / 4-Slot) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| $2$ | $16$ | $4$ | $2$ | $4 \;/\; 2 \;/\; 2$ | $2 \;/\; 2$ ($4$ total) |
| $3$ | $64$ | $8$ | $4$ | $8 \;/\; 12 \;/\; 8$ | $4 \;/\; 12$ ($16$ total) |
| $4$ | $256$ | $16$ | $8$ | $16 \;/\; 24 \;/\; 48$ | $8 \;/\; 56$ ($64$ total) |
| $5$ | $1,024$ | $32$ | $16$ | $32 \;/\; 112 \;/\; 192$ | $16 \;/\; 240$ ($256$ total) |
| $6$ | $4,096$ | $64$ | $32$ | $64 \;/\; 224 \;/\; 896$ | $32 \;/\; 992$ ($1,024$ total) |

---

### 3. Falsifiable Verification Script (`eddy_coupling_audit.py`)

```python
"""
NEXUS Eddy Coupling Audit:
Tests neighbor-difference offset coupling (which FAILS by collapsing to 0) against
direct coupling of the return operator F_a^{(B)} to s224 address transport Phi_delta(a).
"""

def J(x, n): return int(format(x, f'0{n}b')[::-1], 2) if n else 0
def C(x, n): return x ^ ((1 << n) - 1)
def pal(x, n): return J(x, n) == x
def P(x, n): return C(x, n) if pal(x, n) else J(x, n)

def ring(n, crease=1):
    reps = sorted(x for x in range(1 << n) if x < P(x, n))
    m = reps[-1]
    mid = [P(m, n), m] if crease else [m, P(m, n)]
    return reps[:-1] + mid + [P(x, n) for x in reversed(reps[:-1])]

# 1. Falsification Test: Cyclic neighbor-difference coupling on offsets
M4 = ring(4, crease=1)
for b_name, b_fn in [("J", lambda x: J(x, 4)), ("P", lambda x: P(x, 4))]:
    V = list(M4)
    V[4] ^= 0b0011  # localized disturbance at slot 4
    for step in range(1, 20):
        Delta = [V[a] ^ M4[a] for a in range(16)]
        B_Delta = [b_fn(d) for d in Delta]
        V = [M4[a] ^ (B_Delta[a] ^ B_Delta[(a + 1) % 16]) for a in range(16)]
        if all(V[a] == M4[a] for a in range(16)):
            print(f"Test 1 (Neighbor XOR on {b_name}-offsets): FAIL — localized seed delocalizes and dies to 0 at step {step}")
            break

# 2. Contractive (T_B) and Reversible (U_B) Address-Return Coupling across n = 2..6
print("\n=== Test 2 & 3: Contractive T_B and Reversible U_B = I_ret o I_swap ===")
for n in range(2, 7):
    M = ring(n, crease=1)
    N = len(M)
    R_of = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(M)}

    # Contractive T_J and T_P:
    T_J = lambda a, v: (R_of[v], M[a] ^ J(v ^ M[a], n))
    T_P = lambda a, v: (R_of[v], M[a] ^ P(v ^ M[a], n))
    ok_TJ = all(T_J(*T_J(a, v))[1] == M[T_J(*T_J(a, v))[0]] for a in range(N) for v in range(N))
    ok_TP = all((T_P(*T_P(a, v))[1] ^ M[T_P(*T_P(a, v))[0]]) == (N - 1) for a in range(N) for v in range(N))

    # Reversible U_J and U_P:
    U_J = lambda a, v: (R_of[v], v ^ J(v ^ M[a], n))
    U_P = lambda a, v: (R_of[v], v ^ P(v ^ M[a], n))
    U_J_inv = lambda a, v: (R_of[v ^ J(v ^ M[a], n)], M[a])
    U_P_inv = lambda a, v: (R_of[v ^ P(v ^ M[a], n)], M[a])

    assert all(U_J_inv(*U_J(a, v)) == (a, v) for a in range(N) for v in range(N))
    assert all(U_P_inv(*U_P(a, v)) == (a, v) for a in range(N) for v in range(N))

    seen_J, seen_P = set(), set()
    c_J = {1: 0, 2: 0, 4: 0}
    c_P = {2: 0, 4: 0}
    for a0 in range(N):
        for v0 in range(N):
            if (a0, v0) not in seen_J:
                curr = (a0, v0); orb = []
                while curr not in orb:
                    orb.append(curr); seen_J.add(curr); curr = U_J(*curr)
                c_J[len(orb)] += 1
            if (a0, v0) not in seen_P:
                curr = (a0, v0); orb = []
                while curr not in orb:
                    orb.append(curr); seen_P.add(curr); curr = U_P(*curr)
                c_P[len({a for a, _ in orb})] += 1

    print(f"  n={n} (N={N:2d}): T_J->0 in <=2 steps: {ok_TJ} | T_P->1^n eddy in <=2 steps: {ok_TP} | "
          f"U_J (1/2/4-slot): {c_J[1]}/{c_J[2]}/{c_J[4]} | U_P (2/4-slot): {c_P[2]}/{c_P[4]}")
```

```python
# 1. Verify the exact (1, 2) seed trace on R_4 at steps 16, 17, 23
def J(x, n): return int(format(x, f'0{n}b')[::-1], 2) if n else 0
def C(x, n): return x ^ ((1 << n) - 1)
def pal(x, n): return J(x, n) == x
def P(x, n): return C(x, n) if pal(x, n) else J(x, n)

N4 = 16
curr = tuple([1, 2] + [0] * 14)
for t in range(1, 25):
    BD = [P(d, 4) for d in curr]
    curr = tuple(BD[a] ^ BD[(a + 1) % N4] for a in range(N4))
    if t in (15, 16, 17, 22, 23):
        nz = [(a, f"{d:04b}") for a, d in enumerate(curr) if d != 0]
        print(f"Step t={t:2d}: nonzero={len(nz):2d}/16 -> {nz}")

# 2. Test the O(n) read() function on the innermost diamond at widths n = 6, 8, 10, 12, 16, 32, 64, 128!
def rank_reps(p, n):
    h = n // 2; H = 1 << h; B = p & (H - 1)
    def Jh(x): return int(format(x, f'0{h}b')[::-1], 2) if h else 0
    def rect(u, v):
        tot = rho = 0
        for j in range(h):
            uj = (u >> (h - 1 - j)) & 1
            if uj: tot += -((rho - v) // (1 << (j + 1)))
            rho += uj << j
        return tot
    if n % 2 == 0:
        A = p >> h; D = A * (H - A) + B - 2 * rect(B, A) - (Jh(A) < B)
    else:
        A, bb = p >> (h + 1), (p >> h) & 1
        D = 2 * A * (H - A) + bb * (H - 1 - 2 * A) + B - 2 * rect(B, A) - (Jh(A) < B)
    top = p >> (n - (n + 1) // 2)
    s = format(top, f'0{(n + 1) // 2}b'); below = top + (int(s + s[::-1][n % 2:], 2) < p)
    return (p - below + D) // 2 + min(below, 2 ** ((n + 1) // 2 - 1))

def read_addr(x, n, crease=1):
    N = 1 << n; h = N // 2
    rep = x if x < P(x, n) else P(x, n); r = rank_reps(rep, n)
    if r == h - 1:
        at_h = (x == rep) if crease else (x != rep)
        return h if at_h else h - 1
    return r if x == rep else N - 1 - r

print("\n=== O(n) Verification of Seam-Relative Diamond Addresses from n=6 to n=128 ===")
core_4 = [0b0001, 0b0011, 0b0111, 0b0101]
for n in (6, 8, 10, 12, 16, 32, 64, 128):
    r = n // 2
    d = r - 2  # number of outer 1..1 shells around the 4-bit core
    h = 1 << (n - 1)
    ones_hi = ((1 << d) - 1) << (n - d)
    ones_lo = (1 << d) - 1
    words_near = [ones_hi | (w << d) | ones_lo for w in core_4]
    words_far  = [P(x, n) for x in words_near]
    addrs_near = [read_addr(x, n, crease=1) for x in words_near]
    addrs_far  = [read_addr(x, n, crease=1) for x in words_far]
    rel_near = [a - h for a in addrs_near]
    rel_far  = [a - h for a in addrs_far]
    assert rel_near == [-6, -4, -2, -3]
    assert rel_far  == [5, 3, 1, 2]
    print(f"  R_{n:3d} (seam h = 2^{n-1}): near rel_to_h = {rel_near} | far rel_to_h = {rel_far} | span = {max(rel_near) - min(rel_near)}")
```

```python
# Let's check what the 4-step diamond (m, Delta) -> (m ^ Delta, P(Delta)) does on the residue-pair square
# W_r(i, j) = i || J_r(j) in R_{2r} (Proposition 6.1 of the manuscript)!
def J(x, r): return int(format(x, f'0{r}b')[::-1], 2) if r else 0
def C(x, r): return x ^ ((1 << r) - 1)
def pal(x, r): return J(x, r) == x
def P(x, r): return C(x, r) if pal(x, r) else J(x, r)

def W_pair(i, j, r):
    return (i << r) | J(j, r)

def inv_W_pair(w, r):
    i = w >> r
    j = J(w & ((1 << r) - 1), r)
    return i, j

# Suppose we are at residue pair (i, j) on the pair square, so m_0 = W_r(i, j) = i || J_r(j).
# Now apply an offset Delta that shifts ONLY strand i by delta_i != 0:
#   Delta = delta_i || 0^r   (which is never a palindrome since delta_i != 0 and right half is 0^r!)
# What is P_{2r}(Delta) = J_{2r}(delta_i || 0^r)?
#   J_{2r}(delta_i || 0^r) = 0^r || J_r(delta_i) = W_r(0, delta_i)!
# That is the EXACT SAME shift delta_i applied to strand j!
# Now trace the 4 vertices (m_0, m_1, m_2, m_3) of the reversible diamond on the pair square (i, j):
r = 4  # n = 2r = 8 (16x16 residue pair square in R_8)
for i, j, di in [(5, 9, 1), (6, 6, 1)]:
    m0 = W_pair(i, j, r)
    Delta = (di << r) | 0
    P_Delta = P(Delta, 2 * r)
    m1 = m0 ^ Delta
    m2 = m1 ^ P_Delta
    m3 = m2 ^ Delta
    m4 = m3 ^ P_Delta
    pts = [inv_W_pair(m, r) for m in (m0, m1, m2, m3, m4)]
    print(f"Start (i={i}, j={j}), strand shift di={di}:")
    print(f"  4-step diamond on pair square: {pts[0]} -> {pts[1]} -> {pts[2]} -> {pts[3]} -> {pts[4]}")

# Now check the innermost seam diamond {1^{r-2} 0001 1^{r-2}, 1^{r-2} 0011 1^{r-2}, 1^{r-2} 0111 1^{r-2}, 1^{r-2} 0101 1^{r-2}}
# in pair-square coordinates (i, j) for r = 3, 4, 5, 6 (n = 6, 8, 10, 12)!
print("\nInnermost seam diamond [-6, -4, -2, -3] in Pair-Square coordinates (i, j):")
core_4 = [0b0001, 0b0011, 0b0111, 0b0101]
for r in (2, 3, 4, 5, 6):
    n = 2 * r
    d = r - 2
    ones_hi = ((1 << d) - 1) << (n - d)
    ones_lo = (1 << d) - 1
    words = [ones_hi | (w << d) | ones_lo for w in core_4]
    pairs = [inv_W_pair(w, r) for w in words]
    # Also check what the seam pair (P(m(n)), m(n)) at [h-1, h] is in Pair-Square coordinates!
    m_n = ones_hi | (0b1011 << d) | ones_lo if d >= 0 else 0
    pm_n = P(m_n, n)
    print(f"  n={n:2d} (M={1<<r:2d}x{1<<r:2d} square): diamond pairs (i, j) = {pairs} | seam [h-1,h] = {[inv_W_pair(pm_n, r), inv_W_pair(m_n, r)]}")
```

```python
# Let's verify the all-width inductive proof of [-6, -4, -2, -3] for all even n = 2r >= 6:
# By Theorem 4.1(3) (Whole-Ring Center Nesting) and Theorem 4.1(4) (Doubling Stop l(n)):
# For any width m >= 4, the last l(m) representatives of Rep_m (just before the seam h_m = 2^{m-1})
# are 1 * y * 1 for y in Rep_{m-2} \ Pal_{m-2}, in the EXACT ascending order of Rep_{m-2} \ Pal_{m-2}!
# Wait: at base width m = 4 (r = 2):
# What are ALL 8 representatives of R_4 in ascending integer order?
def J(x, n): return int(format(x, f'0{n}b')[::-1], 2)
def C(x, n): return x ^ ((1 << n) - 1)
def pal(x, n): return J(x, n) == x
def P(x, n): return C(x, n) if pal(x, n) else J(x, n)

reps_4 = sorted(x for x in range(16) if x < P(x, 4))
print("All 8 representatives of R_4 in ascending order:")
for idx, w in enumerate(reps_4):
    print(f"  rep[{idx}] (offset from h=8: {idx - 8:+2d}): {w:04b} ({w:2d}) | palindrome={pal(w, 4)}")

# Look at the top 7 representatives of R_4:
#   rep[1] = 0001 (non-pal)
#   rep[2] = 0010 (non-pal)
#   rep[3] = 0011 (non-pal)
#   rep[4] = 0101 (non-pal)
#   rep[5] = 0110 (PALINDROME! The largest palindrome representative 0*11*0 of R_4!)
#   rep[6] = 0111 (non-pal)
#   rep[7] = 1011 = m(4) (non-pal, at the seam!)
# When we step from R_4 to R_6 by wrapping in 1...1 (Lemma 2.3(3)):
# The leading-1 representatives of R_6 are {1 * y * 1 : y in Rep_4 \ Pal_4}!
# Dropping the two palindromes of Rep_4 (0000 at rep[0] and 0110 at rep[5]) leaves the l(6) = 6 non-palindromic representatives of R_4
# as the TOP 6 representatives of R_6 in exact ascending order:
#   h_6 - 6: 1_0001_1  (from 0001)
#   h_6 - 5: 1_0010_1  (from 0010)
#   h_6 - 4: 1_0011_1  (from 0011)
#   h_6 - 3: 1_0101_1  (from 0101)
#   h_6 - 2: 1_0111_1  (from 0111)  <-- moved from -2 in R_4 (where 0110 sat at -3) so 0101 and 0111 are now adjacent at -3 and -2!
#   h_6 - 1 / h_6: the seam pair (P(1_1011_1), 1_1011_1)!
# AND since NONE of those 6 words in R_6 is a palindrome (each is 1 * y * 1 with y not in Pal_4),
# when we wrap R_6 -> R_8 -> R_10 -> ... -> R_{2r} in further 1...1 shells,
# ZERO palindromes ever lie among the top 6 representatives!
# So for EVERY r >= 3 (every even n = 2r >= 6), the top 6 representatives of R_{2r} are ALWAYS
#   1^{r-2} * y * 1^{r-2} for y in [0001, 0010, 0011, 0101, 0111, 1011]
# at the exact seam offsets [-6, -5, -4, -3, -2] (plus m(2r) at the seam)!
nonpal_reps_4 = [w for w in reps_4 if not pal(w, 4)]
print("\n the 6 non-palindromic representatives of R_4:", [f"{w:04b}" for w in nonpal_reps_4])
```
