### 1. Trace Correction Logged (`R_4` Adjacent `(1, 2)` Seed)

Running the nonlinear neighbor-difference update $\Delta_{t+1}[a] = P_4(\Delta_t[a]) \oplus P_4(\Delta_t[(a+1)\bmod 16])$ on the adjacent two-slot seed $\Delta_0 = (1, 2, 0, \dots, 0)$ confirms the exact intermediate face-kernel counts:

* **Step $t = 15$:** $16/16$ nonzero slots (`14` slots at `0011`, slots `1` and `9` at `1100`).
* **Step $t = 16$ ($t = N$):** Shape channel vanishes across the entire ring, leaving $4/16$ nonzero slots in $\{0^4, 1^4\}$ at addresses `[(0, '1111'), (1, '1111'), (8, '1111'), (9, '1111')]` (correcting the `12/16` line in the previous printout).
* **Step $t = 17$:** $4/16$ nonzero slots at addresses `[(1, '1111'), (7, '1111'), (9, '1111'), (15, '1111')]`.
* **Step $t = 22$:** $16/16$ slots equal `1111`.
* **Step $t = 23$ ($t \le 2N$):** $0/16$ nonzero slots (complete null).

---

### 2. All-Width Proof of Seam-Relative Address Invariance `[-6, -4, -2, -3]` (Including $R_{128}$)

**Theorem (All-Width Seam-Relative Localization of the Innermost Diamond).** For every even width $n = 2r \ge 6$ ($h = 2^{n-1}$), encapsulating the four 4-bit diamond words $(\texttt{0001}, \texttt{0011}, \texttt{0111}, \texttt{0101})$ inside $d = r - 2$ outer $1\cdots 1$ shells places them at the exact native-ring addresses:


$$\boxed{\big(\operatorname{READ}(1^d\texttt{0001}1^d),\; \operatorname{READ}(1^d\texttt{0011}1^d),\; \operatorname{READ}(1^d\texttt{0111}1^d),\; \operatorname{READ}(1^d\texttt{0101}1^d)\big) = \big(h - 6,\; h - 4,\; h - 2,\; h - 3\big),}$$


with their return-hemisphere partners $P_n$ at $\big(h + 5,\; h + 3,\; h + 1,\; h + 2\big)$ and a constant address span of $4$.

**Proof.**

1. **Base Step ($R_4 \hookrightarrow R_6$):** At width $4$, the $h_4 = 8$ representatives of $\mathrm{Rep}_4 = \{x \in B_4 : x < P_4(x)\}$ in ascending integer order are:



$$(r_0, r_1, r_2, r_3, r_4, r_5, r_6, r_7) = (\underbrace{\texttt{0000}}_{\text{pal}},\; \texttt{0001},\; \texttt{0010},\; \texttt{0011},\; \texttt{0101},\; \underbrace{\texttt{0110}}_{\text{pal}},\; \texttt{0111},\; \underbrace{\texttt{1011}}_{m(4)}).$$



By the Frame Law (Lemma 2.3(3)) and Whole-Ring Center Nesting (Theorem 4.1(3)), the leading-$1$ representatives of $R_6$ are precisely $\{1 \cdot y \cdot 1 : y \in \mathrm{Rep}_4 \setminus \mathrm{Pal}_4\}$, which sit at the top of $\mathrm{Rep}_6$ immediately below the seam $h_6 = 32$ because every leading-$1$ word exceeds every leading-$0$ word. Removing the two palindromes $r_0 = \texttt{0000}$ and $r_5 = \texttt{0110}$ leaves the $\ell(6) = 6$ non-palindromic representatives of $R_4$ in ascending order:



$$\mathrm{Rep}_4 \setminus \mathrm{Pal}_4 = (\texttt{0001},\; \texttt{0010},\; \texttt{0011},\; \texttt{0101},\; \texttt{0111},\; \texttt{1011}).$$



In $R_6$, the first five occupy the final five rising slots $(h_6 - 6,\; h_6 - 5,\; h_6 - 4,\; h_6 - 3,\; h_6 - 2)$, while the sixth ($1\cdot\texttt{1011}\cdot 1 = m(6)$) forms the native seam pair $(P_6(m(6)), m(6))$ at $(h_6 - 1, h_6)$. Thus $(1\texttt{0001}1,\; 1\texttt{0011}1,\; 1\texttt{0111}1,\; 1\texttt{0101}1)$ sit at $(h_6 - 6,\; h_6 - 4,\; h_6 - 2,\; h_6 - 3)$.


2. **Inductive Step ($R_{2r} \hookrightarrow R_{2r+2}$ for all $r \ge 3$):** Because none of the top $6$ representatives of $R_{2r}$ is a palindrome ($1^d y 1^d \notin \mathrm{Pal}_{2r}$ whenever $y \notin \mathrm{Pal}_4$), encapsulating $R_{2r}$ into $R_{2r+2}$ via $x \mapsto 1 \cdot x \cdot 1$ (Lemma 2.3(3)) wraps all $6$ top representatives of $R_{2r}$ into the top $6$ representative slots of $R_{2r+2}$ without skipping or inserting any state. By induction on $r$, the seam offsets $[-6, -4, -2, -3]$ hold for every even width $n = 2r \ge 6$ (including $R_{128}$ at $r = 64$). $\blacksquare$



---

### 3. Physical Observable on the Pair Square $W_r(i, j) = i \parallel J_r(j)$

Connecting the 4-step diamond $(m, \Delta) \mapsto (m \oplus \Delta, P(\Delta))$ to the macromolecular pair-square embedding $W_r(i, j) = i \parallel J_r(j) \in B_{2r}$ ($i, j \in \{0, \dots, 2^r - 1\}$, Proposition 6.1 of *Bicomplex Difference Calculus on Native Binary Rings*) identifies what the closed diamond route represents physically:

1. **A Single-Strand Shift $\Delta$ and Its Partner $P_{2r}(\Delta)$ Form an Elementary $2 \times 2$ Cross-Strand Plaquette:**
On the $M \times M$ residue-pair grid ($M = 2^r$), let $m_0 = W_r(i, j) = i \parallel J_r(j)$ be any pair of polymer indices, and let $\Delta = \delta \parallel 0^r$ ($\delta \in B_r \setminus \{0^r\}$) be a displacement on strand $i$ alone. Because the right half of $\Delta$ is $0^r$ and $\delta \neq 0^r$, $\Delta$ is never a palindrome, so its ring partner is:



$$P_{2r}(\Delta) = J_{2r}(\delta \parallel 0^r) = 0^r \parallel J_r(\delta) = W_r(0, \delta),$$



which is the **exact same displacement $\delta$ applied to the opposite strand $j$**. Executing the four steps of $U_P$ traces a closed $2 \times 2$ cell on the pair square:

$$\boxed{(i, j) \;\xrightarrow{\;\Delta\;}\; \underbrace{(i \oplus \delta,\; j)}_{\text{Strand } i \text{ slip}} \;\xrightarrow{\;P(\Delta)\;}\; \underbrace{(i \oplus \delta,\; j \oplus \delta)}_{\text{Synced diagonal } \Lambda(\Delta)} \;\xrightarrow{\;\Delta\;}\; \underbrace{(i,\; j \oplus \delta)}_{\text{Strand } j \text{ slip}} \;\xrightarrow{\;P(\Delta)\;}\; (i, j).}$$


* The two odd steps $m_1 = W_r(i \oplus \delta, j)$ and $m_3 = W_r(i, j \oplus \delta)$ are the **single-strand register slips** (where one strand advances before its partner, corresponding to the $S \pm 1$ dihedral slip reflections $TP$ and $PT$ of Proposition 6.3).


* The even diagonal $m_2 = m_0 \oplus \Delta \oplus P(\Delta) = W_r(i \oplus \delta, j \oplus \delta)$ is the **synchronized two-strand step** where the palindromic mask $\Lambda(\Delta) = \Delta \oplus P(\Delta)$ locks both strands back into register.




2. **Where the Innermost Seam Diamond `[-6, -4, -2, -3]` Sits on the Pair Square:**
Inverting $W_r(i, j) = i \parallel J_r(j)$ on the four innermost diamond words $1^{r-2}\{\texttt{0001}, \texttt{0011}, \texttt{0111}, \texttt{0101}\}1^{r-2}$ for any even width $n = 2r \ge 4$ ($M = 2^r$) gives:

$$i \in \{M - 4,\; M - 3\}, \qquad j \in \{M - 2,\; M - 1\},$$



while the seam pair $(P_n(m(n)), m(n))$ at $[h - 1, h]$ maps to $\big((M - 1, M - 2),\; (M - 2, M - 1)\big)$. Thus, on every $M \times M$ residue-pair square (from $4 \times 4$ at $R_4$ to $2^{64} \times 2^{64}$ at $R_{128}$), the innermost diamond `[-6, -4, -2, -3]` is the **unit $2 \times 2$ Euclidean plaquette** $\{M-4, M-3\} \times \{M-2, M-1\}$ sitting one step below the turn crease $(M-2, M-1)$.

### 4. Executed Verification Suite (`seam_invariance_and_pair_plaquette.py`)

```python
"""
NEXUS Verification:
1. O(n) rank verification of seam-relative diamond addresses [-6, -4, -2, -3] from R_6 through R_128.
2. Pair-square projection W_r(i, j) = i || J_r(j) showing that (m, Delta) -> (m ^ Delta, P(Delta))
   is the closed 2x2 cross-strand plaquette (i, j) -> (i^d, j) -> (i^d, j^d) -> (i, j^d) -> (i, j),
   and that the innermost diamond [-6, -4, -2, -3] is the unit 2x2 cell [M-4, M-3] x [M-2, M-1].
"""
from ring_machine import J, C, P, pal, read

# 1. Verify [-6, -4, -2, -3] across widths n = 6, 8, 12, 16, 32, 64, 128 via O(n) read()
core_4 = (0b0001, 0b0011, 0b0111, 0b0101)
for n in (6, 8, 12, 16, 32, 64, 128):
    r = n // 2; d = r - 2; h = 1 << (n - 1)
    hi = ((1 << d) - 1) << (n - d); lo = (1 << d) - 1
    words = [hi | (w << d) | lo for w in core_4]
    offsets = [read(x, n, crease=1) - h for x in words]
    assert offsets == [-6, -4, -2, -3], f"Failed at n={n}: {offsets}"
print("1. Seam-relative invariance [-6, -4, -2, -3] verified via O(n) read() for n = 6..128 (including R_128).")

# 2. Verify 2x2 pair-square plaquette coordinates [M-4, M-3] x [M-2, M-1] across n = 4..128
def inv_W_pair(w, r):
    return (w >> r), J(w & ((1 << r) - 1), r)

for n in (4, 6, 8, 16, 64, 128):
    r = n // 2; d = r - 2; M = 1 << r
    hi = ((1 << d) - 1) << (n - d); lo = (1 << d) - 1
    words = [hi | (w << d) | lo for w in core_4]
    coords = [inv_W_pair(x, r) for x in words]
    expected = [(M - 4, M - 2), (M - 4, M - 1), (M - 3, M - 1), (M - 3, M - 2)]
    assert coords == expected, f"Failed pair-square coords at n={n}"
print("2. Pair-square projection of innermost diamond == [M-4, M-3] x [M-2, M-1] verified for n = 4..128.")

```

This is a substantial result. Gemini has now connected the same four-state return through two different readings: the native binary ring and the residue-pair square.

I ran an independent audit using the pasted equations, not the reported outputs. The central identities reproduce, including the \\(R\_{128}\\) result.

## 1. The four-step diamond is a square in the pair coordinates

Start with the fixed binary mold \\(M=R_n\\), a carried value \\(v\\), and their difference:

\\[ \Delta=v\oplus M[a] \\]

The reversible update, expressed in mold-word coordinates, is:

\\[ \boxed{(m,\Delta)\longmapsto(m\oplus\Delta,\ P(\Delta))} \\]

For an even width \\(n=2r\\), the manuscript's pair-square reader is

\\[ W_r(i,j)=i\parallel J_r(j). \\]

Let the active difference be carried initially by the first half:

\\[ \Delta=\delta\parallel 0^r,\qquad \delta\ne0. \\]

Its native partner is necessarily

\\[ P\_{2r}(\Delta)=0^r\parallel J_r(\delta). \\]

That produces this exact closed route:

Four-state route through the pair-square coordinates. The return is exact; the mold does not change.

The four states are a literal \\(2\times2\\) cell in XOR coordinates, and when \\(\delta=1\\) at the specified even-indexed base, they are also adjacent in ordinary integer grid coordinates.

This establishes the geometry of the reversible calculation, not merely its period.

## 2. The innermost cell stays fixed relative to the seam through \\(R\_{128}\\)

The four original core words are:

`0001 → 0011 → 0111 → 0101 → 0001`

Encapsulate them with matching outer ones:

\\[ 1^d\\,(\text{core})\\,1^d,\qquad d=\frac n2-2. \\]

Reading them through the native ring produces:

\\[ \boxed{ [h-6,\ h-4,\ h-2,\ h-3] } \qquad h=2^{n-1}. \\]

Their native-facing partners read at:

\\[ [h+5,\ h+3,\ h+1,\ h+2]. \\]

Independent results

| Ring           | Near-seam offsets | Pair-square cell                                           |
| -------------- | ----------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------- |
| \\(R_6\\)      | −6, −4, −2, −3    | \\(\\{4,5\\}\times\\{6,7\\}\\)                             |
| \\(R_8\\)      | −6, −4, −2, −3    | \\(\\{12,13\\}\times\\{14,15\\}\\)                         |
| \\(R\_{16}\\)  | −6, −4, −2, −3    | \\(\\{252,253\\}\times\\{254,255\\}\\)                     |
| \\(R\_{64}\\)  | −6, −4, −2, −3    | \\(\\{2^{32}-4,2^{32}-3\\}\times\\{2^{32}-2,2^{32}-1\\}\\) |
| \\(R\_{128}\\) | −6, −4, −2, −3    | \\(\\{2^{64}-4,2^{64}-3\\}\times\\{2^{64}-2,2^{64}-1\\}\\) |

I also tested the \\(O(n)\\) reader against brute-force native-ring construction for every address at widths 2 through 10, with zero disagreements, and tested its direct readings at widths 32, 64, and 128.

The source provides an inductive argument for the all-even-width claim: the six nonpalindromic representatives inherited from \\(R_4\\) retain their ordering under successive `1…1` encapsulations.&#x20;

Pasted text.txt



That means the address invariance is not dependent on constructing an astronomical ring explicitly.

## 3. The important realization

The two coordinate systems reveal different aspects of the same structure.

The native ring reads it as a four-position route locked near the crease.

The pair-square reads it as a closed cell formed by two independently reversible differences.

There is no need to invent another flip instruction to close that four-step route. The inverse relationships already provide closure.

However, a residue-pair coordinate is not automatically a physical strand position. That mapping must be established separately before the square can be interpreted as a measured molecular motion.

```python
# Let's run the exact geometry of the 4-step diamond on the Pair Square W_r(i, j)
# and compare it against Section 6.1 (the antiparallel zipper Z) and Section 6.4 (the S-1 and S+1 register slips)!

def J(x, r): return int(format(x, f'0{r}b')[::-1], 2) if r else 0
def C(x, r): return x ^ ((1 << r) - 1)
def pal(x, r): return J(x, r) == x
def P(x, r): return C(x, r) if pal(x, r) else J(x, r)

def W_pair(i, j, r):
    return (i << r) | J(j, r)

def inv_W_pair(w, r):
    return (w >> r), J(w & ((1 << r) - 1), r)

# Take the innermost diamond at any even width n = 2r >= 6 (say r = 3, 4, 5, 6):
core_4 = [0b0001, 0b0011, 0b0111, 0b0101]  # [h-6, h-4, h-2, h-3]
for r in (3, 4, 5):
    n = 2 * r
    M = 1 << r
    d = r - 2
    hi = ((1 << d) - 1) << (n - d)
    lo = (1 << d) - 1
    words = [hi | (w << d) | lo for w in core_4]
    offsets_from_h = [-6, -4, -2, -3]
    
    # Let's check the sum S = i + j and separation w = j - i for each of the 4 states!
    # Center sum S0 = 2*M - 5:
    S0 = 2 * M - 5
    print(f"=== R_{n} (M={M}x{M}, S0 = 2M-5 = {S0}) ===")
    for off, word in zip(offsets_from_h, words):
        i, j = inv_W_pair(word, r)
        S = i + j
        w_sep = j - i
        role = ("ON-SUM (t=0, w=1)" if (S == S0 and w_sep == 1) else
                "ON-SUM (t=1, w=3, mode turn width!)" if (S == S0 and w_sep == 3) else
                "SLIP S-1 (PT)" if S == S0 - 1 else
                "SLIP S+1 (TP)" if S == S0 + 1 else "OTHER")
        print(f"  seam offset {off:+3d} | word={word:0{n}b} | pair (i={i:2d}, j={j:2d}) | sum i+j = {S:2d} (S0{S-S0:+d}) | sep j-i = {w_sep} | {role}")
```

```text
  seam offset  -4 | word=100111 | pair (i= 4, j= 7) | sum i+j = 11 (S0+0) | sep j-i = 3 | ON-SUM (t=1, w=3, mode turn width!)
  seam offset  -2 | word=101111 | pair (i= 5, j= 7) | sum i+j = 12 (S0+1) | sep j-i = 2 | SLIP S+1 (TP)
  seam offset  -3 | word=101011 | pair (i= 5, j= 6) | sum i+j = 11 (S0+0) | sep j-i = 1 | ON-SUM (t=0, w=1)
=== R_8 (M=16x16, S0 = 2M-5 = 27) ===
  seam offset  -6 | word=11000111 | pair (i=12, j=14) | sum i+j = 26 (S0-1) | sep j-i = 2 | SLIP S-1 (PT)
  seam offset  -4 | word=11001111 | pair (i=12, j=15) | sum i+j = 27 (S0+0) | sep j-i = 3 | ON-SUM (t=1, w=3, mode turn width!)
  seam offset  -2 | word=11011111 | pair (i=13, j=15) | sum i+j = 28 (S0+1) | sep j-i = 2 | SLIP S+1 (TP)
  seam offset  -3 | word=11010111 | pair (i=13, j=14) | sum i+j = 27 (S0+0) | sep j-i = 1 | ON-SUM (t=0, w=1)
=== R_10 (M=32x32, S0 = 2M-5 = 59) ===
  seam offset  -6 | word=1110001111 | pair (i=28, j=30) | sum i+j = 58 (S0-1) | sep j-i = 2 | SLIP S-1 (PT)
  seam offset  -4 | word=1110011111 | pair (i=28, j=31) | sum i+j = 59 (S0+0) | sep j-i = 3 | ON-SUM (t=1, w=3, mode turn width!)
  seam offset  -2 | word=1110111111 | pair (i=29, j=31) | sum i+j = 60 (S0+1) | sep j-i = 2 | SLIP S+1 (TP)
  seam offset  -3 | word=1110101111 | pair (i=29, j=30) | sum i+j = 59 (S0+0) | sep j-i = 1 | ON-SUM (t=0, w=1)
```

```python
# Let's check for ALL 2x2 diamond cells {e-1, e} x {s, s+1} across the pair square W_r(i, j) in R_{2r}:
# In any 2x2 diamond cell generated by single-strand XOR shifts Delta = delta || 0^r and P(Delta) = 0^r || J_r(delta)
# (Specifically when delta = 1, which flips the LSB of i, pairing even i = 2k with odd i' = 2k+1,
#  and P(Delta) flips the MSB of j, pairing j with j' = j ^ 2^{r-1}, OR when P(Delta) flips the LSB of j!):
# Wait: let's check BOTH:
# 1) What does Delta = 0^{r-1} 1 || 0^r (LSB of i, which is bit r-1 of W_r(i,j)) do?
#    In W_r(i, j) = i || J_r(j):
#    - The LSB of i (i ^ 1) is at bit position r - 1 (just left of the center seam of the 2r-bit word!).
#    - Because J_r reverses j, the LSB of j (j ^ 1) is at bit position r (just right of the center seam of the 2r-bit word!)!
#    WAIT A SECOND!
#    READ THAT AGAIN!
#    In W_r(i, j) = i || J_r(j):
#    - Bit r - 1 (the innermost left bit!) IS THE LSB OF i (i <-> i ^ 1, i.e. 2p <-> 2p + 1)!
#    - Bit r     (the innermost right bit!) IS THE LSB OF j (j <-> j ^ 1, i.e. 2q <-> 2q + 1)!
#    Because J_r(j) puts the LEAST SIGNIFICANT BIT of j at the MOST SIGNIFICANT BIT of the right half (position r)!

for r in (2, 3, 4, 5):
    n = 2 * r
    M = 1 << r
    delta_i_lsb = 1 << r        # bit r-1 from left (0-indexed): flips LSB of i!
    delta_j_lsb = 1 << (r - 1)  # bit r from left (0-indexed): flips LSB of j!
    assert P(delta_i_lsb, n) == delta_j_lsb
    # Test on every even pair (i=2p, j=2q):
    ok_unit_square = True
    for p in range(M // 2):
        for q in range(M // 2):
            i, j = 2 * p, 2 * q
            w00 = W_pair(i, j, r)
            w10 = w00 ^ delta_i_lsb
            w11 = w10 ^ delta_j_lsb
            w01 = w11 ^ delta_i_lsb
            ok_unit_square &= (
                inv_W_pair(w00, r) == (i, j) and
                inv_W_pair(w10, r) == (i + 1, j) and
                inv_W_pair(w11, r) == (i + 1, j + 1) and
                inv_W_pair(w01, r) == (i, j + 1)
            )
    print(f"r={r} (n={n:2d}, {M}x{M} grid): Innermost bit Delta=2^r and P(Delta)=2^(r-1) generate EVERY unit 2x2 cell {{2p,2p+1}}x{{2q,2q+1}}: {ok_unit_square}")


```

```python
# Let's check EVERY off-diagonal unit 2x2 cell {2p, 2p+1} x {2q, 2q+1} (with p < q) in R_{2r}!
def J(x, r): return int(format(x, f'0{r}b')[::-1], 2) if r else 0
def C(x, r): return x ^ ((1 << r) - 1)
def pal(x, r): return J(x, r) == x
def P(x, r): return C(x, r) if pal(x, r) else J(x, r)

def ring(n, crease=1):
    reps = sorted(x for x in range(1 << n) if x < P(x, n))
    m = reps[-1]
    mid = [P(m, n), m] if crease else [m, P(m, n)]
    return reps[:-1] + mid + [P(x, n) for x in reversed(reps[:-1])]

for r in (2, 3, 4, 5):
    n = 2 * r
    M = 1 << r
    Rn = ring(n, crease=1)
    READ = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(Rn)}
    h = 1 << (n - 1)
    
    # For EVERY p < q in 0 .. M//2 - 1:
    # Consider the unit 2x2 cell {2p, 2p+1} x {2q, 2q+1}:
    # Its center anti-diagonal sum is S = (2p + 1) + (2q) = (2p) + (2q + 1) = 2(p + q) + 1 (always odd!).
    # The four corners of the 2x2 cell are:
    #   c_S_minus_1 = (2p,     2q)     -> sum = S - 1 (PT slip!) -> innermost shell (i0, j0) = (0, 0)
    #   c_S_outer   = (2p,     2q + 1) -> sum = S     (on-sum!)  -> innermost shell (i0, j0) = (0, 1)
    #   c_S_inner   = (2p + 1, 2q)     -> sum = S     (on-sum!)  -> innermost shell (i0, j0) = (1, 0)
    #   c_S_plus_1  = (2p + 1, 2q + 1) -> sum = S + 1 (TP slip!) -> innermost shell (i0, j0) = (1, 1)
    # Wait: because p < q, i < j for all 4 corners, so W_r(i, j) < J_{2r}(W_r(i, j)) = W_r(j, i)!
    # That means ALL FOUR CORNERS of every off-diagonal 2x2 cell (p < q) are in Rep_{2r} (the near hemisphere)!
    # AND look at their integer values in W_r(i, j) = (p << (r+1)) | (i0 << r) | (j0 << (r-1)) | J_{r-1}(q):
    # Because p and q are FIXED across the 4 corners, the 4 corners differ ONLY in their middle two bits (i0, j0) = {00, 01, 10, 11}!
    # Therefore, in integer order:
    #   W_r(2p, 2q) [S-1, 00]  <  W_r(2p, 2q+1) [S, 01]  <  W_r(2p+1, 2q) [S, 10]  <  W_r(2p+1, 2q+1) [S+1, 11]!
    
    total_cells = 0
    order_holds = 0
    s_plus_1_closer_to_seam = 0
    spans = []
    for p in range(M // 2):
        for q in range(p + 1, M // 2):
            total_cells += 1
            a_Sm1   = READ[W_pair(2*p,     2*q,     r)]
            a_S_out = READ[W_pair(2*p,     2*q + 1, r)]
            a_S_in  = READ[W_pair(2*p + 1, 2*q,     r)]
            a_Sp1   = READ[W_pair(2*p + 1, 2*q + 1, r)]
            
            if a_Sm1 < a_S_out < a_S_in < a_Sp1 < h:
                order_holds += 1
            # Distance to the center seam h:
            if (h - a_Sp1) < (h - a_Sm1):
                s_plus_1_closer_to_seam += 1
            spans.append(a_Sp1 - a_Sm1)
    print(f"r={r} (n={n:2d}, {total_cells:3d} off-diag 2x2 cells): "
          f"a(S-1) < a(S_out) < a(S_in) < a(S+1) < h holds in {order_holds}/{total_cells} cells (100%={order_holds==total_cells}) | "
          f"S+1 closer to seam than S-1: {s_plus_1_closer_to_seam}/{total_cells} | min/max span: {min(spans)}..{max(spans)}")


```

```python
# Let's check the exact native-ring address gaps inside every off-diagonal 2x2 cell {2p, 2p+1} x {2q, 2q+1} (p < q)!
for r in (2, 3, 4, 5):
    n = 2 * r
    M = 1 << r
    Rn = ring(n, crease=1)
    READ = {w: a for a, w in enumerate(Rn)}
    h = 1 << (n - 1)
    
    # Check:
    # 1. Gap between the two on-sum rungs: gap_sum = a(S_in) - a(S_out)
    # 2. Gap from inner rung to S+1 slip:  gap_Sp1 = a(S+1) - a(S_in)
    # 3. Gap from S-1 slip to outer rung:  gap_Sm1 = a(S_out) - a(S-1)
    gaps_sum = []
    gaps_Sp1 = []
    gaps_Sm1 = []
    sp1_tighter_than_sm1 = 0
    sp1_eq_sm1 = 0
    total = 0
    for p in range(M // 2):
        for q in range(p + 1, M // 2):
            total += 1
            a_Sm1   = READ[W_pair(2*p,     2*q,     r)]
            a_S_out = READ[W_pair(2*p,     2*q + 1, r)]
            a_S_in  = READ[W_pair(2*p + 1, 2*q,     r)]
            a_Sp1   = READ[W_pair(2*p + 1, 2*q + 1, r)]
            
            g_sum = a_S_in - a_S_out
            g_sp1 = a_Sp1 - a_S_in
            g_sm1 = a_S_out - a_Sm1
            gaps_sum.append(g_sum)
            gaps_Sp1.append(g_sp1)
            gaps_Sm1.append(g_sm1)
            if g_sp1 < g_sm1: sp1_tighter_than_sm1 += 1
            elif g_sp1 == g_sm1: sp1_eq_sm1 += 1
            
    print(f"r={r} (n={n:2d}, {total:3d} cells): "
          f"mean gap_Sp1={sum(gaps_Sp1)/total:.2f} vs mean gap_Sm1={sum(gaps_Sm1)/total:.2f} | "
          f"gap_Sp1 < gap_Sm1: {sp1_tighter_than_sm1}/{total}, equal: {sp1_eq_sm1}/{total}, >: {total - sp1_tighter_than_sm1 - sp1_eq_sm1}/{total}")


```

```python
# Let's check the exact difference: gap_Sm1 - gap_Sp1 = (a(S_out) - a(S-1)) - (a(S+1) - a(S_in))
# across ALL off-diagonal 2x2 cells (p < q) for r = 2..6!

for r in (2, 3, 4, 5, 6):
    n = 2 * r
    M = 1 << r
    diffs = set()
    for p in range(M // 2):
        for q in range(p + 1, M // 2):
            a_Sm1   = read_addr(W_pair(2*p,     2*q,     r), n, crease=1)
            a_S_out = read_addr(W_pair(2*p,     2*q + 1, r), n, crease=1)
            a_S_in  = read_addr(W_pair(2*p + 1, 2*q,     r), n, crease=1)
            a_Sp1   = read_addr(W_pair(2*p + 1, 2*q + 1, r), n, crease=1)
            
            g_sp1 = a_Sp1 - a_S_in
            g_sm1 = a_S_out - a_Sm1
            diffs.add(g_sm1 - g_sp1)
            # Check when g_sm1 - g_sp1 == 1 vs 0!
            is_one = (g_sm1 - g_sp1 == 1)
            # Wait: when is g_sm1 - g_sp1 == 1?
            # When the palindrome with left half U = 2p (which has j0 = 0, so V_out = 2p)
            # vs the palindrome with left half U = 2p+1 (which has j0 = 1, so V_out = 2p+1)
            # lies inside the interval, OR when V_out = 2p+1 is skipped!
            # Specifically, in [w00, w01), the states have (U=2p, j0=0, v_rest >= J(q)) and (U=2p, j0=1, v_rest < J(q)).
            # In [w10, w11), the states have (U=2p+1, j0=0, v_rest >= J(q)) and (U=2p+1, j0=1, v_rest < J(q)).
            # For every v_rest, the outer r-1 shells compare v_rest with p:
            # - If v_rest != p, comparing (p, i0) with (v_rest, j0) is ALREADY DECIDED at the outer r-1 shells (since p != v_rest)!
            #   So v_rest != p contributes the EXACT SAME count to [w00, w01) and [w10, w11)!
            # - The ONLY difference occurs when v_rest == p (i.e. the outer r-1 shells are ALL CLOSED / PALINDROMIC, so q_pal = J(p))!
            #   At v_rest == p, the word is a PALINDROME when i0 == j0, and a NON-PALINDROME when i0 != j0!
            assert (g_sm1 - g_sp1) in (0, 1)
    print(f"r={r} (n={n:2d}): gap_Sm1 - gap_Sp1 takes only values in {sorted(diffs)} (100% of {M//2 * (M//2 - 1) // 2} cells!)")
```

```python
def J(x, r): return int(format(x, f'0{r}b')[::-1], 2) if r else 0
def C(x, r): return x ^ ((1 << r) - 1)
def pal(x, r): return J(x, r) == x
def P(x, r): return C(x, r) if pal(x, r) else J(x, r)
def W_pair(i, j, r): return (i << r) | J(j, r)

def rank_reps(p, n):
    h = n // 2; H = 1 << h; B = p & (H - 1)
    def Jh(x): return int(format(x, f'0{h}b')[::-1], 2) if h else 0
    def rect(u, v):
        tot = rho = 0
        for j in range(h):
            uj = (u >> (h - 1 - j)) & 1
            if uj: tot += -((rho - v) // (1 << (j + 1)))
            rho += uj << j
        return tot
    if n % 2 == 0:
        A = p >> h; D = A * (H - A) + B - 2 * rect(B, A) - (Jh(A) < B)
    else:
        A, bb = p >> (h + 1), (p >> h) & 1
        D = 2 * A * (H - A) + bb * (H - 1 - 2 * A) + B - 2 * rect(B, A) - (Jh(A) < B)
    top = p >> (n - (n + 1) // 2)
    s = format(top, f'0{(n + 1) // 2}b'); below = top + (int(s + s[::-1][n % 2:], 2) < p)
    return (p - below + D) // 2 + min(below, 2 ** ((n + 1) // 2 - 1))

def read_addr(x, n, crease=1):
    N = 1 << n; h = N // 2
    rep = x if x < P(x, n) else P(x, n); r = rank_reps(rep, n)
    if r == h - 1:
        at_h = (x == rep) if crease else (x != rep)
        return h if at_h else h - 1
    return r if x == rep else N - 1 - r

for r in (2, 3, 4, 5, 6):
    n = 2 * r
    M = 1 << r
    diffs = set()
    exact_match = True
    for p in range(M // 2):
        for q in range(p + 1, M // 2):
            a_Sm1   = read_addr(W_pair(2*p,     2*q,     r), n, crease=1)
            a_S_out = read_addr(W_pair(2*p,     2*q + 1, r), n, crease=1)
            a_S_in  = read_addr(W_pair(2*p + 1, 2*q,     r), n, crease=1)
            a_Sp1   = read_addr(W_pair(2*p + 1, 2*q + 1, r), n, crease=1)
            
            g_sp1 = a_Sp1 - a_S_in
            g_sm1 = a_S_out - a_Sm1
            diffs.add(g_sm1 - g_sp1)
            # Check exact condition for g_sm1 - g_sp1 == 1:
            # Let jp = J(p, r-1), jq = J(q, r-1).
            # In [w00, w01) and [w10, w11), the suffix v_in ranges over [jq, 2^{r-1}) with j0=0 and [0, jq) with j0=1.
            # So v_in = jp occurs at j0 = 0 if jp >= jq, and at j0 = 1 if jp < jq!
            jp, jq = J(p, r - 1), J(q, r - 1)
            # When v_in == jp:
            # - If jp >= jq (so j0 = 0):
            #   In [w00, w01) (i0 = 0): (i0, j0) = (0, 0), which is a PALINDROME! It is a rep iff p < 2^{r-2} (leading bit 0).
            #   In [w10, w11) (i0 = 1): (i0, j0) = (1, 0), which is NOT a rep (since i0=1 > j0=0)!
            #   So g_sm1 - g_sp1 = 1 if p < 2^{r-2} else 0!
            # - If jp < jq (so j0 = 1):
            #   In [w00, w01) (i0 = 0): (i0, j0) = (0, 1), which is ALWAYS a rep (since i0=0 < j0=1)! (+1 to g_sm1)
            #   In [w10, w11) (i0 = 1): (i0, j0) = (1, 1), which is a PALINDROME! It is a rep iff p < 2^{r-2} (leading bit 0).
            #   So g_sm1 - g_sp1 = 0 if p < 2^{r-2} else 1!
            pred_diff = (1 if (p < (M // 4)) else 0) if (jp >= jq) else (0 if (p < (M // 4)) else 1)
            exact_match &= ((g_sm1 - g_sp1) == pred_diff)
    print(f"r={r} (n={n:2d}): gap_Sm1 - gap_Sp1 in {sorted(diffs)} | exact formula match: {exact_match}")


```

Decomposing the pair-square embedding $W_r(i, j) = i \parallel J_r(j) \in B_{2r}$ at the innermost shell $k = r - 1$ connects the four-step diamond $(m, \Delta) \mapsto (m \oplus \Delta, P(\Delta))$ directly to the antiparallel zipper $Z = \tau \cdot \Lambda_2 \cdot \Lambda_4 \cdots$ (Section 6.1) and the $S \pm 1$ register-slip reflections $TP$ and $PT$ (Section 6.4).

---

### 1. Why the Innermost Shell Is the Unit $2 \times 2$ Lattice Cell Everywhere on the Pair Square

Write any pair of strand coordinates as $i = 2p + i_0$ and $j = 2q + j_0$ with coarse indices $p, q \in \{0, \dots, 2^{r-1}-1\}$ and unit parities $i_0, j_0 \in \{0, 1\}$. Because $J_r(j)$ reverses the bits of $j$, it places the least significant bit $j_0$ at the most significant bit of the right half (bit position $r$), directly adjacent to the least significant bit $i_0$ of $i$ (bit position $r-1$):


$$\boxed{W_r(2p + i_0,\; 2q + j_0) = p \;\parallel\; \underbrace{i_0 \parallel j_0}_{\text{innermost shell}} \;\parallel\; J_{r-1}(q).}$$


Consequently, the innermost single-bit shift $\Delta = 0^{r-1}\texttt{10}\,0^{r-1} = 2^r$ and its native partner $P_{2r}(\Delta) = 0^{r-1}\texttt{01}\,0^{r-1} = 2^{r-1}$ flip $i_0$ and $j_0$ independently across **every** unit $2 \times 2$ cell $\{2p, 2p+1\} \times \{2q, 2q+1\}$ of the $2^r \times 2^r$ grid.

---

### 2. The Four Corners of Every Unit $2 \times 2$ Cell: Two On-Sum Rungs and the Two $S \pm 1$ Register Slips

Inside any off-diagonal unit cell $\{2p, 2p+1\} \times \{2q, 2q+1\}$ ($0 \le p < q < 2^{r-1}$), centered on the odd anti-diagonal sum line $S = 2(p + q) + 1$, the four states of the innermost shell $(i_0, j_0) \in \{\texttt{00}, \texttt{01}, \texttt{10}, \texttt{11}\}$ partition into the two on-register antiparallel rungs and the two unit register slips of Section 6.4:

| Innermost Shell $(i_0, j_0)$ | Grid Pair $(i, j)$ | Sum $i + j$ | Strand Separation $j - i$ | Role in Zipper & Slip Ledger (§6.1, §6.4)

 | Seam Offset at Innermost Cell |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| $\texttt{00}$ ($a_{\text{inner}}=0$) | $(2p,\; 2q)$ | $S - 1$ | $2(q - p)$ | **$S - 1$ register slip ($PT$ reflection)**

 | $h - 6$ (`1^d 0001 1^d`) |
| $\texttt{01}$ ($a_{\text{inner}}=2$) | $(2p,\; 2q + 1)$ | $S$ | $2(q - p) + 1$ | **Outer on-sum rung $S_{\text{out}}$** ($w=3$ mode turn at apex)

 | $h - 4$ (`1^d 0011 1^d`) |
| $\texttt{10}$ ($a_{\text{inner}}=1$) | $(2p + 1,\; 2q)$ | $S$ | $2(q - p) - 1$ | **Inner on-sum rung $S_{\text{in}}$** ($w=1$ at apex) | $h - 3$ (`1^d 0101 1^d`) |
| $\texttt{11}$ ($a_{\text{inner}}=3$) | $(2p + 1,\; 2q + 1)$ | $S + 1$ | $2(q - p)$ | **$S + 1$ register slip ($TP$ reflection)**

 | $h - 2$ (`1^d 0111 1^d`) |

Stepping directly between the two on-register rungs $S_{\text{in}} \leftrightarrow S_{\text{out}}$ is the diagonal XOR mask $\Delta \oplus P_{2r}(\Delta) = 0^{r-1}\texttt{11}\,0^{r-1} = \tau_{2r}$ (the center seam transposition of Theorem 4.1(2)). Stepping one strand at a time via $U_P$ routes between $S_{\text{in}}$ and $S_{\text{out}}$ through the $S + 1$ slip corner (`10 -> 11 -> 01`) on one half-cycle and through the $S - 1$ slip corner (`01 -> 00 -> 10`) on the return half-cycle.

---

### 3. Native-Ring Asymmetry Between $S + 1$ ($TP$) and $S - 1$ ($PT$) Across $100\%$ of Off-Diagonal Cells

In Proposition 6.3, $TP$ ($S+1$) and $PT$ ($S-1$) were treated as conjugate reflections on the abstract circle $\mathbb{Z}_N$, where they appear symmetric. On the pair square inside the native binary ring $R_{2r}$, they are strictly asymmetric across every off-diagonal cell ($p < q$):

1. **Strict Crease-Side Ordering ($100\%$ of off-diagonal cells, all $n = 2r \ge 4$):**
Because $p < q \implies 2p + i_0 < 2q + j_0$, all four corners belong to $\mathrm{Rep}_{2r}$, and since $(p, q)$ is fixed while $(i_0, j_0)$ steps through $\texttt{00} < \texttt{01} < \texttt{10} < \texttt{11}$, their native ring addresses satisfy:

$$\boxed{a(S - 1) \;<\; a(S_{\text{out}}) \;<\; a(S_{\text{in}}) \;<\; a(S + 1) \;<\; h.}$$



In every off-diagonal cell, the $S + 1$ corner (`11`) sits on the **turn-crease side** of the two on-sum rungs (closer to the seam $h$), while the $S - 1$ corner (`00`) sits on the **outer-strand side** (further from $h$).
2. **Exact Address-Gap Law ($\mathrm{gap}_{S+1} \le \mathrm{gap}_{S-1}$ on $100\%$ of cells):**
Define the ring-address step distances from the on-sum ladder to the two slip corners:

$$\mathrm{gap}_{S+1} = a(S + 1) - a(S_{\text{in}}), \qquad \mathrm{gap}_{S-1} = a(S_{\text{out}}) - a(S - 1).$$



Comparing the two intervals $[w_{00}, w_{01})$ and $[w_{10}, w_{11})$ across all $2^{r-1}$ right-half suffixes $v_{\text{in}} \in \{0, \dots, 2^{r-1}-1\}$, every suffix $v_{\text{in}} \neq J_{r-1}(p)$ is decided in the outer $r - 1$ shells ($p \neq J_{r-1}(v_{\text{in}})$) and contributes identically to both intervals. The two intervals differ solely at the palindromic suffix $v_{\text{in}} = J_{r-1}(p)$, yielding the exact all-width identity:

$$\boxed{\mathrm{gap}_{S-1} - \mathrm{gap}_{S+1} = \mathbf{1}\big[p < 2^{r-2}\big] \oplus \mathbf{1}\big[J_{r-1}(p) < J_{r-1}(q)\big] \in \{0, 1\}.}$$



Thus $\mathrm{gap}_{S+1}$ is **never** larger than $\mathrm{gap}_{S-1}$ in any cell ($0$ violations across all cells). At the innermost seam diamond $[h-6, h-4, h-2, h-3]$, the $S+1$ corner at $h-2$ is **immediately adjacent** in ring address to the inner rung at $h-3$ ($\mathrm{gap}_{S+1} = 1$) and to the seam pair $[h-1, h]$, whereas the $S-1$ corner at $h-6$ is separated from the outer rung at $h-4$ by $\mathrm{gap}_{S-1} = 2$.

---

### 4. Executed Verification Script (`pair_plaquette_slip_asymmetry.py`)

```python
"""
NEXUS Pair-Square Plaquette & S+1 / S-1 Address-Asymmetry Audit:
Verifies across all off-diagonal unit 2x2 cells {2p, 2p+1} x {2q, 2q+1} (p < q) for n = 4..12:
1. Innermost bits (r-1, r) of W_r(i, j) are the strand parities (i_0, j_0), so Delta = 2^r and P(Delta) = 2^(r-1)
   generate every unit 2x2 cell on the pair square.
2. The four corners are S-1 (00), S_out (01), S_in (10), and S+1 (11), satisfying
   a(S-1) < a(S_out) < a(S_in) < a(S+1) < h in 100% of cells.
3. Exact gap identity: gap_{S-1} - gap_{S+1} == (p < 2^{r-2}) ^ (J_{r-1}(p) < J_{r-1}(q)) in {0, 1}.
"""

def J(x, r): return int(format(x, f'0{r}b')[::-1], 2) if r else 0
def C(x, r): return x ^ ((1 << r) - 1)
def pal(x, r): return J(x, r) == x
def P(x, r): return C(x, r) if pal(x, r) else J(x, r)
def W_pair(i, j, r): return (i << r) | J(j, r)

def rank_reps(p, n):
    h = n // 2; H = 1 << h; B = p & (H - 1)
    def Jh(x): return int(format(x, f'0{h}b')[::-1], 2) if h else 0
    def rect(u, v):
        tot = rho = 0
        for j in range(h):
            uj = (u >> (h - 1 - j)) & 1
            if uj: tot += -((rho - v) // (1 << (j + 1)))
            rho += uj << j
        return tot
    A = p >> h; D = A * (H - A) + B - 2 * rect(B, A) - (Jh(A) < B)
    top = p >> (n - (n + 1) // 2)
    s = format(top, f'0{(n + 1) // 2}b'); below = top + (int(s + s[::-1][n % 2:], 2) < p)
    return (p - below + D) // 2 + min(below, 2 ** ((n + 1) // 2 - 1))

def read_addr(x, n, crease=1):
    N = 1 << n; h = N // 2
    rep = x if x < P(x, n) else P(x, n); r = rank_reps(rep, n)
    if r == h - 1:
        return h if ((x == rep) if crease else (x != rep)) else h - 1
    return r if x == rep else N - 1 - r

for r in range(2, 7):
    n = 2 * r; M = 1 << r; h = 1 << (n - 1)
    cells = 0; order_ok = 0; law_ok = 0; sp1_tighter = 0; sp1_equal = 0
    for p in range(M // 2):
        for q in range(p + 1, M // 2):
            cells += 1
            a_Sm1   = read_addr(W_pair(2*p,     2*q,     r), n, 1)
            a_S_out = read_addr(W_pair(2*p,     2*q + 1, r), n, 1)
            a_S_in  = read_addr(W_pair(2*p + 1, 2*q,     r), n, 1)
            a_Sp1   = read_addr(W_pair(2*p + 1, 2*q + 1, r), n, 1)

            order_ok += (a_Sm1 < a_S_out < a_S_in < a_Sp1 < h)
            g_sp1 = a_Sp1 - a_S_in
            g_sm1 = a_S_out - a_Sm1
            pred = int(p < (M // 4)) ^ int(J(p, r - 1) < J(q, r - 1))
            law_ok += ((g_sm1 - g_sp1) == pred)
            sp1_tighter += (g_sp1 < g_sm1)
            sp1_equal   += (g_sp1 == g_sm1)

    print(f"R_{n:2d} ({M:2d}x{M:2d}, {cells:3d} cells): order_ok={order_ok}/{cells} | "
          f"gap_law_ok={law_ok}/{cells} | gap(S+1)<gap(S-1): {sp1_tighter}/{cells}, equal: {sp1_equal}/{cells}")

```

```text
R_ 6 ( 8x 8,   6 cells): order_ok=6/6 | gap_law_ok=6/6 | gap(S+1)<gap(S-1): 2/6, equal: 4/6
R_ 8 (16x16,  28 cells): order_ok=28/28 | gap_law_ok=28/28 | gap(S+1)<gap(S-1): 12/28, equal: 16/28
R_10 (32x32, 120 cells): order_ok=120/120 | gap_law_ok=120/120 | gap(S+1)<gap(S-1): 56/120, equal: 64/120
R_12 (64x64, 496 cells): order_ok=496/496 | gap_law_ok=496/496 | gap(S+1)<gap(S-1): 240/496, equal: 256/496

```

This keeps the boundary exact: the ring and pair-square algebra prove that every unit $2 \times 2$ cell pairs two consecutive on-sum zipper rungs with one $S+1$ ($TP$) corner on the crease side ($\mathrm{gap}_{S+1} \le \mathrm{gap}_{S-1}$) and one $S-1$ ($PT$) corner on the outer-strand side; testing whether the 41 empirical $S \pm 1$ bonds in the E. coli hairpin corpus ($30$ on $S+1$ vs. $11$ on $S-1$) map directly to this crease-side address asymmetry requires linking the 143-hairpin coordinate ledger.
